# Findings ledger

One row per finding, kept for ever. `id` is `A<area>-<n>`. Status: `open`, `fixed <commit>`, or
`accepted: <reason>`. The fixture column names the fixture in the suite once fixed, or holds the
reviewer's words and literals until then. Rounds before this ledger are recorded in
`Docs/FunctionalityVerification/15_DECOMPILER_STEP5.md`.

| id | area | severity | finding | fixture | status | found | closed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| A2-1 | 2 | P3 | `ldc Rm,SR` and `ldc.l @Rm+,SR` keep SR bits the hardware does not have: the spec masks with `0x0FFF0FFF` (`superh.sinc`, `ldc rm_08_11,sr` and `ldc.l @rm_08_11+,sr`), Mednafen with `0x3F3` (T S I3-I0 Q M; `sh7095_ops.inc:1653-1664` and `1673-1686`, every SR load ends `z->SR &= 0x3F3`). | A2-2 | 2 | P2 | `rte` restores SR after its delay slot; the hardware restores it before. The spec runs `delayslot(1); pc = _pc; sr = _sr;` (`superh.sinc`, `rte`); Mednafen sets `z->SR` from the stack, then calls `DelayBranch` (`sh7095_ops.inc:1735-1757`, SR at line 1746). So a slot that reads SR (`movt`, `addc`, `subc`, `div1`, a conditional...) sees the old T, S, Q, M, and a slot that writes SR (`sett`, `clrt`, `cmp`, `shll`, `ldc`, `div0s`, ...) has its write overwritten by the stacked value. The usual `rte ; nop` is unaffected; `rte` with a useful slot is the pattern. 1,159 of 2,000 `rte` cases differ (SR in 750 with an ordinary slot, a register in about 60, memory in 13, MAC in 2). Coordinator: re-triaged from P1: wrong only for an rte slot that reads or writes T; a spec fix, area 2 round 1. | Fixed by patch 0019 (`sr = _sr` before `delayslot(1)`, as 0006 did for PR). `SaturnExplorerSh2DecodeTests`: shapes `rte; sett`, `rte; clrt`, `rte; movt r2`, form `rte`. Mutation (SR after the slot): the three shapes fail, 371 of 2,000 `rte` cases. | fixed 4d37623 | area 2 oracle | area 2 round 1 |

| A2-4 | 2 | P3 | A PC-relative load or `mova` in the delay slot of a taken `bt/s`, `bf/s`, `jmp`, `jsr`, `rts`, `braf`, `bsrf` or `rte` reads relative to the slot's own address + 4; the hardware reads PC as the branch target + 2 (`sh7095.inc:4757-4777` `UCDelayBranch` and `4696-4725` `DelayBranch` set PC to the target, the post-switch `z->PC += 2` at `sh7095_ops.inc:2082` follows, and `MOV.W/L @(disp,PC)` and `MOVA` use that PC: `sh7095_ops.inc:109-126, 521`). | A2-5 | 2 | P1 | Translating an instruction can return the p-code of its delay slot instead of its own. `Sleigh` keeps parse trees in a ring of 2 (`DisassemblyCache`, `sleigh.cc:576-582`: `parser_cachesize = 2` because SH-2's `delayslot(1)` is one byte of slot, so `maxdelayslotbytes` is 1; lookups hash on `offset & 31`). If a branch is translated while its tree is cached but its slot's is not, the slot lookup takes the ring's next entry, and that entry is the branch's own tree (`getParserContext` does not advance the ring on a hit). The branch is then built from the slot's parse: `rts` becomes its slot alone, `bt/s` loses the condition and the jump, with no warning. The trigger sequence is: the slot address translated first (as a branch target, say), then the branch, then one other instruction, then the branch again (`872, 870, 874, 870` with `bt/s` at 870 and `sett` at 872: 7 p-code ops, then 3). It also came up unprompted in 1 of 60,000 random `bt/s` executions. Every delayed branch form (`bra`, `bsr`, `braf`, `bsrf`, `jmp`, `jsr`, `rts`, `rte`, `bt/s`, `bf/s`: 5,632 cells) shows it. A vendored-engine defect, not the spec; whether the decompiler's flow can reach the sequence (it needs a slot that is also a jump target and a second translation of the branch, as when flow runs again after a jump table is recovered) is not established, so P1 only by the rule for a silent wrong result; the coordinator may re-triage. Areas 4 and 10 should look at it. Coordinator: kept P1 until its reach is settled: area 10 (patch review) establishes whether flow following can translate a cached branch after a slot miss; if it can, patch the ring size; if it cannot, mark accepted with the proof. | Settled: reachable from the decompiler, fixed by patch 0020 (the cache is least recently used: a hit makes the entry the newest). `ReturnCheck` (`Sh2Decompiler.cpp`) translates a function again after the flow; a function that starts with a delayed branch whose slot is also a jump target, with a loop back to it, translated slot, branch, one plain instruction, branch, and the branch came out as its slot alone: the signature was withheld ("an indirect jump it does not resolve"). The flow itself translates each address once. `SaturnExplorerSh2DecodeTests`: `TestRecycledTreeInDecompiler` (the function, words `A002 7201 412B 0009 8BFA 012A 89F9 89F9 000B 0009` at `06009000`) and `TestTranslationHistory` (5,632 cells). Without the patch: the signature is withheld and 5,632 of 5,632 cells differ. A larger ring is no fix (a tree can always be the oldest). Record: "Area 2, round 1", section 5. | fixed 4d37623 | area 2 oracle (found by a stray `next` difference in the random run) | area 2 round 1 |

| A5-3 | 5 | P2 | An access narrower than the register it reaches is printed as a write or read of that part of the register, with no note, where the hardware does something else. A byte write to a VDP2 or VDP1 register writes the whole 16-bit register, its other byte taken from whatever was last on the data bus (Mednafen `vdp2.c` `RW_W8` -> `RegsWrite(A, *DB)`; `vdp1.c` `VDP1_Write8_DB` -> `WriteReg((A - 0x100000) >> 1, DB)`; `sh7095.inc` `SH7095_BSC_BusWrite_u8` keeps the other byte lane of `DB`). The SH-2's 32-bit on-chip modules (0xFFFFFF00-0xFFFFFFFF: divider, DMAC, BSC) do not take a word as half a register: `OnChipRegWrite_u16` writes the whole of DVDNT at 0xFF04 and starts the division (`SH7095_DIVU_S32_S32`), and writes nothing at 0xFF06; `OnChipRegRead_INLINE_u16` returns the low 16 bits for 0xFF04 and 0xFF06 alike; a byte access raises a CPU address error (`SH7095_SetPEX(z, SH7095_PEX_CPUADDR)`) and writes the byte doubled. `HardwareNotes` compares widths only for the keyed watchdog and for an access covering two registers, although the comment on the disabled rules in `Sh2Decompiler.cpp` says "VDP2 refuses byte writes". The ledger is right (the instruction's width); the line's meaning is not. P2 as a missing caution; the coordinator may read it as "wrong width with no warning" (P1): the printed width is the instruction's, the effect's is the register's. Corpus: no wrong instance (the only partial register accesses are `SH2_BCR1._2_2_`, word reads at 0xFFFFFFE2 in 2 functions of each save state, which Mednafen answers with the low half, as printed). | Words from 0x06009000, the pool among them; `Fixture` literal offset and value. **a** (VDP2 byte write): `{ 0x06009000, { 0xD102, 0xE003, 0x2100, 0x000B, 0x0009, 0x0009, 0x25F8, 0x0001 }, 0x0C, 0x25F80001 }` (`mov.l @(pool),r1 ; mov #3,r0 ; mov.b r0,@r1`). Expected: the same ledger and a caution on 0x06009004 that VDP2 takes no byte write (the 16-bit VDP2_TVMD is written whole, its other byte undefined). Literal 0x25F80000 the same (`VDP2_TVMD._0_1_ = 3;`). **b** (VDP1): literal 0x25D00001: `VDP1_TVMR._1_1_ = 3;`, no note; expected the same caution. **c** (on-chip word write): `{ 0x06009000, { 0xD102, 0xE003, 0x2101, 0x000B, 0x0009, 0x0009, 0xFFFF, 0xFF06 }, 0x0C, 0xFFFFFF06 }`: `SH2_DVDNT._2_2_ = 3;`, no note; expected a caution that the 32-bit register takes no 16-bit write there. Fixed: `HardwareRegister::narrow` (`NarrowAccess`) in the map's access-kind table: VDP1 and VDP2 registers `WholeOnByte`, 32-bit on-chip registers from 0xFFFFFF00 `OnChipLong` (the bus state controller `OnChipKeyedLong`), 16-bit ones there `OnChipWord`; `NarrowAccessEffects` says what the access does and `HardwareNotes` adds it as a caution on the instruction, naming the register and the width. | A5-4 | 5 | P2 | A 16-bit write at the even address of an SMPC register writes that register, and the output calls it RAM. The SMPC is a byte device on the low byte lane: Mednafen `ss.c` `BusRW_DB_CS0_u16_W1` does `SMPC_A = (A & 0x7F) >> 1` and a `SMPC_Write(...)` that no `A & 1` test guards, so `mov.w r0,@r1` at 0x2010001E writes SMPC_COMREG with r0's low byte: an SMPC command. The map names SMPC registers 1 byte wide at the odd addresses only, so the line is `Ram2010001e = 0x19;` with no note. Same decode: a byte write at an even SMPC address does nothing (`BusRW_DB_CS0_u8_W1` writes only when `A & 1`), printed as a write with no note; a 32-bit write at 0x2010001C is two 16-bit bus writes (SMPC register 0x0E, then COMREG), printed `Ram2010001c = 0x19;`; a 16-bit read at 0x20100060 returns `0xFF00` ORed with SMPC_SR (`BusRW_DB_CS0_u16_W0`), printed `Ram20100060`. The markup is silently cut short, something later in the Print phase fails on it with an exception that is none of `std::bad_alloc`, `std::exception` or `LowlevelError` (it is caught by the final `catch (...)`), and `Decompile` reports `Failed` with the text `unexpected exception` instead of `out of memory`. The engine is still marked damaged (the `catch (...)` arm sets it), so the process is safe and the next request is Unavailable; only the message is wrong. 8 allocations of 513 bytes (the stream's buffer growth): mailbox 21067, 21073, 32980, 32988; jumptable 31762, 31771, 31774, 31779. | `kPins`: mailbox print #0 513 B #0 and 1025 B #0, jumptable print #0 513 B #0; removing the host's `markup.exceptions(badbit)` fails these 3 cells with "unexpected exception" | fixed d41c806 | area 6 oracle (`review/6-lifecycle`) | area 6 round 1 |
| A6-5 | 6 | P2 | The same stream behaviour in the Actions and Flow phases hides an allocation failure completely: Ghidra builds warning text and messages in `std::ostringstream` (`Funcdata::removeUnreachableBlocks`, `funcdata_block.cc:408`; `FlowInfo::recoverJumpTables`, `flow.cc`) and the failed 513-byte buffer growth only truncates the text. `Decompile` returns `Ok`, no engine damage is recorded, nothing is reported, and the output is wrong: mailbox allocation 18426 turns `/* WARNING: Removing unreachable block (ram,0x0600a48c) */` into `/* WARNING: Removing unreac */` in the shown pseudocode (the caution a reader relies on to learn that code was removed). Allocations 9188 (mailbox) and 8305, 20591 (jumptable) are swallowed the same way with the output unchanged this time, which is luck. Fix: set `exceptions(std::ios::badbit)` on the engine's message streams (a patch to the few `ostringstream` sites that feed comments and warnings), or have the host reject a result whose warning text is cut. | `kPins`: mailbox actions #0 and #1 513 B #0 (#1 is the truncated `WARNING: Removing unreac`), jumptable followFlow #0 and #1 513 B #0; reverting `error.hh`/`filemanage.hh` (the engine's `ostringstream`) fails these 4 cells | fixed d41c806 | area 6 oracle (`review/6-lifecycle`) | area 6 round 1 |
| A6-6 | 6 | P3 | A cancel raised in the tail of the first pass's Actions is carried into the second pass. `Sh2Decompiler.cpp` documents that a cancel "requested anywhere in a phase is then honoured in that phase, never carried into the next one's work", and does check at the end of Flow and before Print, but the first pass does not print, so `runPass` returns before its `checkCancel()`. The flag waits through the second pass's Teardown and Init (Architecture construction has no check-point: about 7,970 of 19,913 allocations of the fixture1 run, 40%) and is honoured in Flow of the second pass: `cancelledIn` is Flow for a flag raised in Actions. Found on fixture1 (allocations 9897 and 9898, the last two before the first Teardown) and on mailbox by the timer family (48,400 us into the run, ASan). Cost: one extra Architecture build before a Stop or a budget cancel takes effect. Fix: a `checkCancel()` before `return std::string()` in the non-printing branch. | default subset, `cancel-alloc fixture1` around the first pass's Teardown entry (allocations 10662-10663 in the numbering of this commit); removing the `checkCancel()` of the pass boundary fails these 2 cells | fixed d41c806 | area 6 oracle (`review/6-lifecycle`) | area 6 round 1 |
| A6-7 | 6 | P3 | An allocation failure while an `Architecture` is being built or initialised leaks what was built so far. Under ASan with leak detection 2,742 of 3,877 sampled cells of the mailbox fixture and 2,705 of 3,610 of the jumptable one (every cell with the failure in an Init phase, allocations 27 to about 8,000 of each Architecture) leak between a few hundred bytes and about 30 KB. Bounded: the engine is refused after the first in-engine failure, so at most one such leak per process. | A2-9 | 2 | P1 | `trapa` is translated as a plain call, so in the decompiler's view the 8 bytes it pushes are never popped: every stack access after a `trapa` is 8 bytes off and reads what `trapa` pushed. The spec (`superh.sinc:1886-1897`, `trapa`) does `r15 -= 4; *r15 = sr; r15 -= 4; *r15 = inst_next; call [*(vbr + imm*4)]`, and the default prototype keeps r15 across a call (`superh.cspec`: r15 in `<unaffected>`, `extrapop="0"`), so after it r15 is the entry value minus 8. On the hardware the handler returns with `rte`, which pops PC and SR (`sh7095_ops.inc:1739-1743`, `z->R[15] += 4` twice) after `TRAPA` (`sh7095_ops.inc:1900-1906`) pushed them through `Exception` (`sh7095.inc:4809-4813`, `z->R[15] -= 4` twice): r15 is back where it was. So a register saved before a system call and restored after it reads back the return address `trapa` pushed, a word just above the stack pointer reads the stacked SR, and the output says nothing about it (the only text is the withheld signature's "an indirect call it does not resolve", which is about lending, not the body). The instruction itself is right: the differential run agrees on the two pushes, their order (SR first), the pushed PC (`inst_next`), the vector read (VBR + imm*4) and the access widths. Reach: code that saves registers around a `trapa` (a system-call wrapper); rare in Saturn games, which call the BIOS through `jsr`. | Fixed by patch 0025. `Sh2DecompilerTests` `TestCallsAndTraps`: the ledger's fixtures a and b word for word and `50F1`, a trap with a local, two traps in a row, the entry r15; each also run on the p-code emulator (handler `rte ; nop`) and the evaluated return compared with r0. `Sh2SemanticsTests` `TestTrapaStack`. Mutation (the pop removed): every trapa fixture and oracle check fails; the decode test, the semantics test and the generator pass (the emulator ends an instruction at its call and never runs the op, so the differential run cannot see it). Record: `15_DECOMPILER_STEP5.md`, "Areas 2 and 3, round 2". | fixed 5944580 | area 2 round 2 review | areas 2 and 3 round 2 |
| A2-10 | 2 | P1 | A register the default prototype neither keeps nor kills reads, after a call, as the value it had before the call. `superh.cspec` names r2 and r3 `killedbycall` (r0 and r1 are the output) and r8-r15 and GBR `unaffected`; r4-r7, SR (T, and S, Q, M), MACH and MACL are in neither list, so the engine gives the call an unknown effect on them: it does not fold the old value through the call, but it merges the values before and after into one variable, and the C text then reads as if the call left them alone (`xVar1 = 5; func(); return xVar1;`). A callee may change every one of them: any `clrt`, `sett` or compare writes T (`sh7095_ops.inc:1636-1639` `CLRT`, `SH7095_SetT` in each compare), any `mov` writes r4-r7 (`MOV_IMM_REG`, lines 96-104), and r4-r7 and T are scratch in the SH calling conventions. A hand-written routine that returns a status in T (`bsr check ; nop ; bt fail`) is the common shape, and the output then shows the branch decided by the T from before the call. A listed register shows what is wanted: r2 prints `return extraout_r2;`, an unknown. Lent prototypes do not change it: `ApplyPrototype` (`Sh2Decompiler.cpp:179-191`) sets `pieces.model = arch.defaultfp`, the same effect lists. Whether a callee may change MACH and MACL is an ABI question; they print the same way. A calling-convention matter (area 3 may own the fix in the cspec), listed here because T is an SR flag the ledger cannot see. | Fixed by patch 0026: r0, r1, r4-r7, MACH, MACL and SR in `killedbycall`, VBR in `unaffected`. `Sh2DecompilerTests` `TestCallsAndTraps`: the ledger's a and b word for word, r4-r6, MACL, MACH and r1 (with a lent void callee), recursion through r1 and r7 (round-3 addendum), VBR across a call, lent prototypes, controls (r2, r8, an argument kept in r8) and a pinned limit (a callee that keeps r4 is not told apart); the oracle runs each on the emulator. Mutation: each list entry dropped fails the fixtures for it. Corpus: 87 of 839 and 136 of 1,554 functions differ, 74 and 112 call sites gain an argument. Open consequence: a caller relying on a callee keeping r4-r7 reads `extraout_r4` and is not lent a signature (needs per-callee effects from the engine; see the record). Record: `15_DECOMPILER_STEP5.md`, "Areas 2 and 3, round 2". | fixed 5944580 | area 2 round 2 review | areas 2 and 3 round 2 |

A closed panel asks nothing, so when the view returns to the same identity before the panel is shown again (scrub a recorded frame and resume the live view, or scrub away and back to the same frame; a BIOS image dropped and chosen again; a function start set and cleared), `q == mAsked` in `Update` and nothing is submitted. The function the user chose never appears. The previous one stays on screen with the stale badge, whose tooltip says "its replacement is shown when it is ready". There is no "decompiling...", no note, and nothing outstanding, until Enter or Refresh. While the panel is shown this cannot happen: the `Update` that discards the answer asks the new question at once. | `SE_MODEL_SCRIPT="hold; select A; hide; scrub 1; wait; resume; show; wait" SE_MODEL_WORLD=C,A build/bin/SaturnExplorerDecompilerModelTests <scratch>`: NOT REFRESHED after operation 7 (shown C, wanted A, pending 0, discarded 1). | A3-3 | 3 | P2 | A lent callee proved to change a register the default model holds unaffected (r8-r14, r15, GBR, VBR) is still taken to keep it. `ReturnCheck` clears the register's bit in `CallEffects.preserved`, and `LendEffects` (patch 0028) only marks kept registers among r2-r7, SR, MACH and MACL unaffected. It never kills a register the proof shows changed. So the caller reads the value from before the call, and the caller's own signature is lent on that value. Without lending, the same text is the cspec's convention (r8-r14 and GBR callee-saved). Once lent, the host holds a proof to the contrary and ignores it. Hand-written Saturn routines use custom conventions. Corpus image 008, lending run with callees first (as `TestGameLending`): `060647EC` (`mov #0x40,r0 ; add r14,r0 ; mov.w @r8+,r1 ; mov.w @r8+,r2 ; shll8 r1 ; mov.w @r8+,r3 ...`, which takes r8 and r14 and advances r8) is lent `void f(void)` and called in a loop by `0606538E`, itself lent. `06064228` (`mov.l @(lit),r0 ; ldc r0,gbr ; ... mov.b r0,@(d,gbr)`) sets GBR and is lent to `060641E0`, which is lent to `06063F2E`. Of 1,004 lent prototypes, 295 miss one of these registers. Most are r14/r15 of entries found at a `sts.l pr,@-r15` one instruction after the real prologue (`mov.l r14,@-r15`), an artifact of that heuristic. 23 callers are lent such a callee. | `TestCallsAndTraps`, "A3-3 (round 3)": `r8 set by the callee`, `r9 ...`, `r14 ...` (the caller reads `extraout_rN` and is withheld on it; with the effects not lent, by the test hook, it prints `return 5;`), `r8 saved and restored by the callee` (proved kept: `return 5;`, lent), `GBR set by the callee`, `VBR set by the callee`, `r15 lowered by the callee` (the callee's constant r15 delta is its extrapop: the caller's pop reads the callee's word, `xStack_8`, and both are lent), `r15 moved by an argument` (the delta varies: not lent, nor its caller). Generator: check 10 over GBR, VBR, MACH and MACL (they start at values of their own), new check 12 (`kept-read`: the text never reads a register a lent callee changes as the value it had on entry), grammar 2 callees that leave r12-r14, GBR, VBR, MACH or MACL changed and callers that read the register right after the call. `TestGameLending` prints the callers of such callees | fixed fdc68ff, b1d783a, fb2a517 (`LendEffects` kills the r8-r14, GBR and VBR a lent callee was proved to change; a constant r15 offset is lent as the callee's extrapop and applied by `ReturnCheck`, one that differs between returns withholds; "Area 3, round 3" in 15_DECOMPILER_STEP5.md) | areas 3 and 4 round 3 review | area 3 round 3 fixes |
| A3-4 | 3 | P2 | A callee that writes through a structure-return pointer in r2 is not seen to write the caller's local: the read after the call is folded to the value stored before it, lent or not. The SH ABI of both compilers (Renesas SHC; GCC, `SH_STRUCT_VALUE_REGNUM` 2) passes the address of a returned structure in r2. The cspec has no input in r2, so `mov r15,r2` is dead, the local's address never reaches the call, and the engine takes the call to leave the local alone. The host knows better and does not say so. The callee's `CallEffects.storesThrough` has r2's bit, which is why the caller's `ReturnCheck` (lent) clobbers its saved PR and withholds "a return not to its caller". On `mailbox` it is 840, 842 and 844. Every cell, the pins' census and `--sites` run the next run (N+1), but they index it with the baseline's sizes. `--cell` then prints coordinates (`PIN-AT`) for an allocation it does not fail. When the markup crosses a 1,024-byte decoder buffer between the two runs, every later allocation of Print is off by one. The census then names run N+1's allocation k under the baseline's size at k, and nothing checks that the size matches. So `--sites` reports a 32-byte list node as `endIngest`'s 1-byte buffer, a pin's `site ok` can be another allocation of the same function, and a fixture tuned with `--sites` to reach a path misses it in every cell. That is how this round's endIngest probe first missed. The text is identical across the runs, and so are the tokens. | `Warm` takes the baseline from a forked run N+1 (the run every cell makes); the cell checks that the allocation it fails is the baseline's size; the pins' census runs the baseline's sequence and checks the size at each pin's coordinates and the whole run, size for size; a site names its ordinal (`#n`: the n-th allocation of the pin's size at the site in the phase entry); `--sites` takes a site, lists ordinals and marks an allocation of another size; `--diffruns` shows the markup growing. Triage: a product over-report, not a wrong text, so **P3** (set down from the provisional P2; the text is right and the status errs to the safe side). Function 0 loads 0xC0000010 into r6 and runs on into function 1, a switch on r6 (`cmp/hi #4,r6 ; bt default ; shll2 r6 ; mova ; mov.l @(r0,r6),r1 ; jmp @r1`). The guard always branches to the default for that constant, but the flow is made before the actions can know it: the table is read at the index 0xC0000010 shifted left by 2 (the literal word after the table, 0x25FE0008 in the shrunk module), flow follows it into uncaptured memory, halts there, and the actions remove that block ("Removing unreachable block (ram,0x25fe0008)"). The text is `return 0x6001030;`, right, and was reported "flows into uncaptured memory at 0x05FE0008 (SCU); the function is cut off there", with the signature withheld "truncated". Fix (host): a cut at the start of a block the engine says it removed is said as a path followed and removed ("which the analysis proved cannot be taken and removed: the function is not cut off there", an engine-kind flow warning) and does not set `truncated`. Left as it was, on purpose: the engine's own flag for the halt it made stays, so the signature is still withheld ("bad instruction data"), and the engine still follows a table that a constant guard excludes (upstream's `isReachable` marks it partial and, with one table in the flow, completes it): ending that flow is an engine change with nothing to gain but the lending of this rare shape. Bytes that do not decode in a removed block are still reported as bad data. Observed, not filed: `bodyBytes` spans to the removed block's address (536,702,985 here); nothing reads it. | `TestRoutedSeeds` (`a4_16`: no cut, no `truncated`, the warning once, `return 0x6001030;`, still withheld; the cut tests of `TestFlowUncertainty` and `TestTruncatedFlow` are the controls); `TestRoutedPins`: the seed and its shrunk module. | A6-30 | 6 | P2 | The first request of a process can fail inside the engine library's one-time start-up and leave global state that a later request trusts. `SaturnArchitecture::StartLibrary` set `started` before the spec bundle was materialised and `ok` before `ghidra::startDecompilerLibrary` had run; the library fills process-global tables (attribute and element ids, the capability lists, the spec search path) by appending, so it can be neither re-run nor finished after a failure. An allocation failure before any phase entry is outside the host's exception boundary for a damaged engine (`mWorkPhase` is Idle), so the engine was not marked damaged. Three outcomes, by where the allocation fell (swept over every allocation of the first request): (1) inside the materialisation (allocations 0-62 with the bundle extracted, 0-103 without): the first request was Failed("out of memory") and every later one Unavailable with an empty explanation (167 cells); (2) inside `startDecompilerLibrary` (66-506, 107-547): Failed("out of memory") with the engine not damaged, and for allocations 63-106 (index 100, 56 bytes, in `AttributeId::initialize` among them) the next request Failed("No print languages registered") from the half-filled list (6 cells); the others reached the engine with tables of unknown content (882 cells); (3) a Disassemble marked the engine damaged for an out of memory in the materialisation, which touches no engine global. Found by the external review at f0684b9 (finding 2); the warm fault matrix, which warms the engine before it injects, could not see the class. | `SaturnExplorerDecompilerFaultTests` families `cold` (Decompile first) and `cold-disasm` (Disassemble first), job labels `cold mailbox`, `cold-disasm mailbox`: cells fork from a parent that never ran the engine, once with the spec bundle already extracted and once with an empty config directory, allocation k of the first request fails, then a second Decompile and a Disassemble. One cell: `SaturnExplorerDecompilerFaultTests <scratch> --cell cold mailbox <2k + (empty config ? 1 : 0)>`. Invariants: a failure in the materialisation is transient (Failed or a stopped listing, the next requests correct, the engine neither damaged nor refused); a failure inside `startDecompilerLibrary` (found by the failing allocation's stack, not by index) refuses the engine from the first request on with "the engine's library did not finish starting (...); restart Saturn Explorer to decompile again" for Decompile (Unavailable) and Disassemble alike, without entering the engine; each of the three steps (`AttributeId::initialize`, `ElementId::initialize`, `CapabilityPoint::initializeAll`) must be reached by the sweep. On 979edd2: 1,055 of 1,105 and 882 of 1,101 cells fail (167 `Unavailable ''`, 6 `No print languages registered`, 882 plain out of memory) and the coverage check fails; fixed: 0 of 1,105 and 0 of 1,101, 882 cells refused with the reason. Mutations (each fails the family): blind retry of the interrupted start (883 + 883 cells), a failed materialisation committed with no explanation (167 + 167), the stage committed before the materialisation (173 + 173), the refusal without the restart text (883 + 883), Disassemble letting the exception reach its damage boundary (167 in `cold-disasm`). `SE_FAULT_EXIT=static` passes too. See Docs/Decompiler/GPTReview/Response-f0684b9.md. | fixed b964b91 (test dfadefb): `StartLibrary` is a state machine, Fresh / Ready / Refused / Interrupted. A materialisation failure leaves it Fresh and is retried; a failure inside `startDecompilerLibrary` makes it Interrupted for good and every call answers "Decompiler unavailable: the engine's library did not finish starting (out of memory); restart Saturn Explorer to decompile again" (the first included); `Disassemble` calls it outside its damage boundary. Not retried: the library's registration is not idempotent. DECOMPILER_GUIDE.md has the text. | external review f0684b9, finding 2 | external ledger review response |

| A5-50 | 5 | P2 | A byte write to VDP2 colour RAM is printed as an insert into the old memory: `iRam25f00000 = CONCAT11(iRam25f00000._0_1_,3);` keeps the high byte of the entry from memory. So the value the decompiler propagates for the high byte is not the one the hardware stores; the following `mov.w @r1,r0` is printed as reading it back. This is a wrong value, not a missing note: it is the subcase (4) of A5-16 that was accepted as 'ordinary memory to the decompiler, no volatile operation to attach a caution to'. Raised by the external review at f0684b9 (the A5-16 remark) as P2; the output cannot be shown to be right, since the fold depends on the old CRAM contents the hardware ignores. | A5-51 | 5 | P2 | A pointer merged from register literals and then moved between bus partitions with a mask (`(uint4)puVar1 & 0x1fffffff`, `(uint4)puVar1 or 0x20000000`: the cached / cache-through idiom, SBL's `CACHE_THROUGH`) is not followed: `PointerResolver` (`Sh2Decompiler.cpp`) walks COPY, CAST, MULTIEQUAL, PTRSUB, PTRADD, INT_ADD and INT_SUB only, so INT_AND / INT_OR fall to `default` (`others`), no constant survives, and `HardwareNotes` finds no candidate and says nothing at all: no cache caution, no read effect, no write effect. The A5-37 class through the partition bits instead of an offset. | A5-52 | 5 | P2 | Two addresses of the same work-RAM bytes are two memories to the engine's data flow: a store through one alias is forwarded to a later load through the same alias past a store through another alias, so the load is folded away and its value printed wrong. `xRam26001000 = 5; xRam06001000 = 7; return 5;` where the hardware returns 7: 0x06001000 and 0x26001000 are the same word (the cached and cache-through partitions; `sh7095.inc` `MemWrite` case 0 writes the cache and falls into case 1's `ExtBusWrite`; the final read is through the cache-through partition, so no cache state changes the answer), and so are 0x26100000 and 0x26000000 (high work RAM, `ss.c` `BusRW_DB_CS3_*`: `A & 0xFFFFF`), 0x2E001000 and 0x26001000 (bits 28-27 not decoded), 0x20201000 and 0x00201000 (low work RAM). DESIGN.md 6.1 folds "the HWRAM 1 MiB repeat" for the image; the data flow does not know it. The read is not in the ledger (an access to ordinary memory at a constant address is folded by design) and no oracle compares the printed value: the generator's emulator does alias work RAM (`Env::ReadValue`, `ram[(a + i) & 0xFFFFF]`: the run returns 7), but outside grammar 3 (register-only) nothing compares the value the text returns. A user who marks the range volatile is safe (`VolatileAliases` covers every alias); the default request is not. Corpus: one word named through both partitions in 3 functions (`hwram007` 06003C46 and 060340FE, `hwram008` 0607513E, the slave's idle loop: `xRam26003304 = 0; ... Ram46003304 = 0; ... while (pcRam06003304 == (code *)0x0)`, and `hwram008` 060716A8: `(*pcRam260b72e8)(); xRam060b72e8 = 0;`); none forwards a store across the alias, so no corpus line is wrong today. (The idle loop is right by accident: an engine that knew the alias would fold the poll to the 0 stored before it, the mailbox case, unless the purge at 0x46003304 is taken as the line's barrier.) Possibly the engine's memory model (areas 2/4); filed here as the mirror map's. | `a5_52a`, `a5_52_repeat`, `a5_52_bit27`, `a5_52_low`, `a5_52_long`, `a5_52_same`, `a5_52_apart`, `a5_52_idle`, `a5_52_purge`; generator rule 14 (`alias.words`) and `SE_GENERATED_MUTATE=alias` | fixed 90d80c9 (follow-ups 3a71c6f, 6802560, 17d8e27, 3376de3) | area 5 round 7 review | area 5 round 7 fix |

| A2-31 | 2 | P2 | Whether a value left in r0 is returned depends on what sits in `rts`'s delay slot. `nop` is `r0 = r0` in the spec (`superh.sinc`, `:nop`: "FIXME: intentional nop ... do this to suppress warning"), and that COPY is a top ancestor whose only use is the RETURN, so `Funcdata::ancestorOpUse`/`onlyOpUse` (`funcdata_varnode.cc:1916-2090`, upstream) accept the return trial. With any other slot (a callee-saved pop `mov.l @r15+,r14`, `add #n,r15`, `sett`, `mov r0,r1` ...) the trial is the value's own definition, and `onlyOpUse` rejects it when the value has any other use: a CBRANCH (via `cmp/eq #imm,r0`, `tst r0,r0`, `cmp/pz r0`), a LOAD or STORE address or a stored value, a CALL, a copy to r1 that reaches the RETURN; `ancestorOpUse` also returns false outright for a CALL output ("a call is never a good indication"). The return is then dropped, the text prints `void`, and the signature is **lent** `void`, often with the parameter that was the value dropped too. SH compilers fill the slot of the epilogue with the last pop, and r0 is the only register `cmp/eq #imm` and `tst #imm` take, so the shapes are ordinary compiled code: `return g(p);`, `p = g(x); if (p) *p = 0; return p;`, `return ++*p;`, `if (x == 5) *p = v; return x;`. The binary of a `void` function that leaves such a value in r0 is the same, so which reading is right is not decidable from the code; the defect is that an unrelated slot instruction decides it and the answer is lent as known (A3-5/A3-14's decision for a dropped r0 is "withheld"). Both oracles miss it: the generator's returning functions never return a call's result or a compared/stored r0 through a filled slot (grammar 2, 3,000 seeds: 0 findings, "slot pops=1997"), and the printed-C differential counts the leaf forms `void-return` (A2-34) and has no calls. Corpus: image 008 with lending (`SE_GAME_LEND`), 131 functions (some entries twice, at +0 and +2) print `void` and end in `func_0x...(...); return;` to a callee whose own text returns a value, every one with a non-nop rts slot; 41 of them lent. With the slot replaced by a nop and nothing else changed, 324 of 397 `void` functions of image 007 and 778 of 963 of image 008 print a return (`slotscan`, scratch). Mednafen: `NOP` (`sh7095_ops.inc`) does nothing. Neighbours: A3-19, A3-20 (dropped returns, lent `void`, other mechanisms). | **a** (wrapper, compiled shape): `SE_GENERATED_WORDS="2FE6 4F22 B003 0009 4F26 000B 6EF6 6042 000B 0009" SE_GENERATED_FUNCS="0/0/e,e/e/14" SE_GENERATED_RETURNS="1,1" build/bin/SaturnExplorerSh2GeneratedTests <dir>` (`mov.l r14,@-r15 ; sts.l pr,@-r15 ; bsr g ; nop ; lds.l @r15+,pr ; rts ; mov.l @r15+,r14`; g at 0600900E is `mov.l @r4,r0 ; rts ; nop`, lent `known, 1 param(s), returns`). Current: `void func_0x06009000(xunknown4 *param_1) { func_0x0600900e(param_1); return; }`, `prototype: known, 1 param(s), void`; generator `[n:return-dropped]`. Control: `2FE6 4F22 B004 0009 4F26 6EF6 000B 0009 6042 000B 0009` (`FUNCS="0/0/10,10/10/16"`, the pop before `rts`, `nop` in the slot): `xVar1 = func_0x06009010(param_1); return xVar1;`, lent `returns`, 0 findings. As a `CallFixture`: caller `{ 0x06009000, { 0x2FE6, 0x4F22, 0xB003, 0x0009, 0x4F26, 0x000B, 0x6EF6 } }`, callee at `0600900E` `{ 0x6042, 0x000B, 0x0009 }`. **b** (`p = g(x); if (p) *p = 0; return p;`): `2FE6 4F22 B008 0009 2008 8901 E100 2012 4F26 000B 6EF6 0009 6042 000B 0009`, `FUNCS="0/0/16,18/18/1e" RETURNS="1,1"`: `void`, lent, `return-dropped`; control with `4F26 6EF6 000B 0009` returns `pxVar1`. **c** leaves, one word apart (`7F00` = `add #0,r15`, no effect): `6042 7001 2402 000B 7F00` (`return ++*p`) `void func(int4 *param_1)`, lent; `6043 2052 000B 7F00` `void`, lent, where `6043 2052 000B 0009` returns `param_1`; `6043 8805 8B00 2652 000B 7F00` (`if (x == 5) *p = v; return x;`) `void`, 3 params, lent, where `... 000B 0009` returns `param_1`; `6053 7001 000B 6103` (`rts ; mov r0,r1`, the A2-22 copy-to-r1 shape) `void func_0x06009000(void)`, 0 params, lent; generator with `FUNCS="0/0/8" RETURNS="1"`: `return-dropped`. Expected: the slot does not decide the return (no `r0 = r0` in `nop`, or the copy marked incidental) and, where the engine's heuristic drops an r0 the function writes on every path, the signature withheld, not lent `void`. Oracle: generator check 9 (`return-dropped`) rejects every fixture given the intent. Fixed as `0045-sleigh-nop-writes-nothing-rte-sr-after-slot.patch` (`nop` writes nothing: it was `r0 = r0`, the last write of r0 and its only use, so a function ending `rts ; nop` returned its value and the same function with any other instruction in the slot did not) and `0044-engine-round5-printed-value-fixes.patch` (1) (`Funcdata::ancestorOpUse`: a RETURN of the output of a call whose prototype is locked and returns a value is a return, whatever else the value is used for; upstream's "a call is never a good indication of a single op use" is a rule for parameters and decided the return of a wrapper). The rest of the decision is in "Area 2, round 5" in 15_DECOMPILER_STEP5.md: the slot no longer decides and the engine's own rule (a value with another use is scratch) holds in every slot; withholding every `void` signature over an r0 written on every path was measured and rejected (lent signatures 600 -> 365 in image 007 and 984 -> 553 in image 008); what is left is A2-42. The nop had also hidden a class the host never withheld, r0 computed from r0's own entry value and then used again (`shll r0 ; cmp/eq #1,r0 ; rts`): `InputsInReturn` now withholds it in every slot, for any value computed from r0's entry value (`add r4,r0 ; xor r0,r1 ; rts`, generator seed 12623, has a parameter in it too), and `xor r0,r0` / `x - x` are 0, not a read (`Sh2Decompiler.cpp`, `ReturnCheck::Return` and its interpretation of two ops; the corpus's one casualty, image 008's 06073C7C, was the zeroing idiom). | A5-63 | 5 | P3 | (Area 6's invariant, found through A5-52's retry.) A cancel raised in the teardown between two passes of an alias retry is carried through the next pass's Init into its Flow instead of being honoured in Actions (A6-35: a cancel is honoured where it was raised, never carried into the next phase). The first pass has its `checkCancel()` after its locals and Architecture are gone; the retry (`aliasRetry`, a printing pass that found words named twice returns before it prints and gives way to another) has none, because `runPass` returns the empty string and the loop starts the next pass. A request that has a first pass (`registerCallees`) finds the words in it and never retries, which is why the common case and the fault suite's fixtures are right; a request without one (`registerCallees = false`, the fault suite's `lean`, or a seeded request) retries once per growth of the set. Fault suite cell on the fixture below: `FAIL cancel-enter aliasl 3: invariant: the cancel is honoured where the design says -- raised in teardown#3: expected Cancelled-in-Actions but got status 2 cancelledIn followFlow` (phases: init, followFlow, actions, teardown, init, followFlow, actions, print, teardown; cells 0-2 and 4-8 pass, the non-lean fixture with the same words passes all nine). The default subset has no fixture that retries. The result is still `Cancelled` and carries no lines: the phase named is wrong, and a hook raising at that entry sees the next pass's Init start. P3. A check-point after the pass's teardown for a retried pass, as for the first, closes it. Fixed in the host: `DecompileInto` calls `checkCancel()` after a printing pass that gives way to another (`aliasRetry`), as after the first pass (A6-35), so a cancel raised in that teardown is honoured in Actions. Fault suite: fixtures `aliasl` (lean, retries) and `alias` (first pass, no retry) in the default subset's cancel-enter family; the alloc sweeps run on them too. | A5-68 | 5 | P3 | (Corrects A5-57's premise, and adds the case it was reaching for.) The VDP1 frame buffer's 8 bpp rotate address scramble does not make two addresses one byte, or one address two: `FBA = (FBA and 0x1FF) or ((FBA << 1) and 0x3FC00) or ((FBA >> 8) and 0x200)` is a permutation of the 18 address bits (checked over all 2^18 offsets: no collisions, and the 512 KiB window repeats it exactly as the 256 KiB repeat of the plain mode does), bit 0 stays in place so a word keeps its two bytes, and a byte written at A is read back from A in either mode. So within a mode a store forwarded to a load of the same address is right, and A5-53's `MemoryBytesOf` repeat is exact for both modes; the claim that a forwarded store 'can still be wrong' there does not hold. What does break the data flow is a register write between the store and the load that re-addresses the memory: `VDP1_TVMR` (`vdp1.c` `WriteReg` case 0: `VDP1_TVMR = value and 0xF`, immediate; the scramble applies to every later CPU access) and `VDP2_RAMCTL` (`vdp2.c` `RegsWrite` case 0x0E: `CRAM_Mode = (V >> 12) and 0x3`, immediate; modes 2 and 3 map entry `cri` to `((cri >> 1) and 0x3FF) or ((cri and 1) << 10)`, so an entry stored in mode 0 is not where mode 3 reads it). Both fold today: `xRam25c80200 = 5; VDP1_TVMR = 3; return 5;` where the load reads the byte the plain mode stored at 0x400 of the buffer, not the 5 (offset 0x200 is 0x400 scrambled); `xRam25f00004 = 0x1234; VDP2_RAMCTL = 0x3000; return 0x1234;` where mode 3 reads entry 1, which the mode 0 store never wrote. The same class as A5-52 (the data flow keys memory by address and the address means something else after the write), at the price of a rare shape: P3. Fix: a write to either register makes the memories it re-addresses volatile in the function, or says it (A5-57 proposed the same). The decision on A5-57 is the coordinator's: its text calls a bijection an alias. Fixed (the first of A5-57's two proposals): a write to VDP1_TVMR or VDP2_RAMCTL anywhere in the function makes every access it makes to the memory the register re-addresses (the frame buffer, colour RAM) volatile in that function (`ConstAccess::remaps`, `NewAliasedRanges`), so no store is carried across the write. A barrier by position was not taken: the data flow has no hook for one, and a function that writes a mode register while using the memory is rare. | `a5_68_tvmr`, `a5_68_ramctl` | fixed 34806ec | area 5 round 8 | area 5 round 8 fix |

The printed-C differential cannot tell a swapped byte name of an unaffected register: `unaff_r8_b1` and `unaff_r8_b2` get the same value. `CalleeSaved(r)` is `0xA0000000 + r * 0x01010101`, so its bytes 1 and 2 are equal for every r8-r14 (r8: `A8 08 08 08`), and the harness reads `unaff_rN_bK` as `(CalleeSaved(N) >> 8K) & 0xFF`. A mutant of `registerPieceSuffix` (`database.cc`, patch 0044 (3)) that numbers the bytes 1 and 2 the other way round (`if (lsb == 1) lsb = 2; else if (lsb == 2) lsb = 1;` before `if (lsb != 0)`) prints `(char)((uint4)param_3 >> 8) == unaff_r8_b2` and `(char)((uint4)param_3 >> 0x10) == unaff_r8_b1`, the wrong byte of the register under each name, and the printed-C differential says `agrees` for `cmp/str r6,r8` and `cmp/str r6,r14`, and the unit suite's A2-37 pins (`Has("_b1") && Has("_b2") && Has("_b3")`) pass. It is not only the callee-saved registers: `cmp/str r6,r1` (`in_r1_b1`, `in_r1_b2`, whose values are drawn) also `agrees` under the mutant, because a `cmp/str` is an OR of four byte compares and 32 inputs of which the edge values have equal bytes and the rest random 32-bit values (a byte pair equal with probability 1/256) almost never make exactly one of the swapped compares decide; `cmp/str r1,r2` swaps both sides alike. Only `r15` (`kStackTop`, bytes that differ) is caught. The names are the user's only way to tell which byte of a register a text means, and A2-25/A2-37 are about exactly that; no oracle pins their order. P3: the engine's numbering is right today (the unmutated engine's 165 piece-naming functions of the round 6 scan, every register r0-r14 and five instruction shapes, all `agrees`), the instrument is blind to a regression. | `Sh2GeneratedTests` printed-C: `CalleeSaved` with four different bytes, 44 inputs that share exactly one byte with a register a function may compare, r15 given the stack top the run gives it; the four hand words `286C 000B 0029;2E6C 000B 0029;216C 000B 0029;2F6C 000B 0029` agree unmutated and all four mismatch under the `registerPieceSuffix` mutant of the row | fixed 0fa9c49 (oracle only; the harness had also given the text r15 = CalleeSaved(15) where the run had the stack top, which agreed only while no input matched a byte of either) | area 2 round 6 | area 3 round 7 fixes |

| A5-70 | 5 | P3 | A5-60's closure of a pointer's own bitwise values is lost when the operation is conditional, or one of two in a turn of the loop, and both then print a wrong or missing note. Cause (`PointerResolver`, `Sh2Decompiler.cpp`): `Walk` closes `selfOps` at a MULTIEQUAL (`CloseSelfOps`) over the constants that merge itself holds. (1) An inner join of the loop body holds none (the first value sits at the loop head's other input), so `if (c) p = p and mask;` leaves `selfOps` in a merge with an empty set, which is cleared with nothing closed, and the head's merge sees only the first value; the usual order `read; update; loop` has the read use the head's phi, which is where it goes wrong (the same update before the read is closed: control below). (2) `Bitwise` takes an operand that is a node with `selfOps` and a cycle but no constant for `onlyCycle` and builds its result from the new operation alone, so the earlier operation of the same turn is dropped. (3) A step on the other path (`Shift` of a cycle value) makes the merge `others`, and `CloseSelfOps` then closes nothing. Fixtures, each replayable with `SE_GENERATED_WORDS="<words>"` (the literal pool is among the words; base 0x06009000). (a) `mov.l lit,r1 (0x25F80004); mov.l lit,r3 (0x1FFFFFFF); mov #3,r4; L: mov.w @r1,r0; tst r0,r0; bt skip; and r3,r1; skip: dt r4; bf L`: the emulator reads 0x05F80004 (the cached partition: a cache read) and 0x25F80004; the output is `puVar1 = &VDP2_TVSTAT; do { if (*puVar1 != 0) { puVar1 = (uint2 *)((uint4)puVar1 & 0x1fffffff); } ... }` with the one note `VDP2_TVSTAT: a read clears EXLTFG`, certain, no `if the pointer holds`, no cache caution; the generator replay reports `[n:cache-read-pointer-unsaid]`. Control (a'): the same with the conditional mask before the read (`tst r4,r4; bt skip; and r3,r1; skip: mov.w @r1,r0`) says `if the pointer holds 0x05F80004 (VDP2_TVSTAT): a read of VDP2_TVSTAT through the cached partition is a cache read ...`. (b) two operations in one turn, `p = (p and 0xFFFFFFF0) xor 4` from 0x25F80008 (VDP2_HCNT): the reads are 0x25F80008, then 0x25F80004 (VDP2_TVSTAT) for ever; the output prints `puVar2 = &VDP2_HCNT; ... puVar2 & 0xfffffff0 ^ 4` and no note at all (the TVSTAT read is unsaid, and the pointer is taken for certain). (c) the same with `and 0xFFFFFFF9`, `xor 4` from 0x25F80006 (VDP2_VRSIZE): the reads are 0x25F80006, then 0x25F80004; the output says `if the pointer holds 0x25F80002 (VDP2_EXTEN): VDP2_EXTEN: a read latches HCNT and VCNT while EXLTEN is 0`, a register the code never reads (the xor alone closes 0x25F80006 to 0x25F80002), and nothing of TVSTAT: a false note. (d) a mask on one path and a step on the other, `tst r0,r0; bt step; and r3,r1; bra join; nop; step: add #4,r1; join: dt r4; bf L`: the emulator reads 0x05F80004 and 0x25F80004, 0x25F80008, 0x25F8000C; the notes are `if the pointer holds 0x25F80004 (VDP2_TVSTAT) (it may hold other values too)` and the stepped note, with no cache caution (`[n:cache-read-pointer-unsaid]`). (e) a step and a mask in one turn, the commonest form, `L: mov.w @r1,r0; add #4,r1; and r3,r1; dt r4; bf L` from 0x25F80004 with r3 = 0x1FFFFFFF: the emulator reads 0x25F80004, 0x05F80008 and 0x05F8000C; the output is `puVar2 = (uint2 *)((uint4)(puVar2 + 2) & 0x1fffffff)` with the two notes of the stepped pointer (`if the pointer holds 0x25F80004 (VDP2_TVSTAT) (it may hold other values too)`, `the pointer is stepped in a loop from 0x25F80004: ... not said`) and no word on the cached partition (`[n:cache-read-pointer-unsaid]` twice). Expected: (a) and (d) say the cache caution under `if the pointer holds 0x05F80004 (VDP2_TVSTAT) (it may hold other values too)`, and (e) says that the pointer is moved into the cached partition (a stepped pointer's addresses are not worked out, but the mask's partition is known: the stepped note names it, `the iterations after the first read through the cached partition 0x05F8xxxx too: a cache read ...`); (b) names 0x25F80008 and 0x25F80004 (`VDP2_TVSTAT: a read clears EXLTFG`) under `if the pointer holds ... (it may hold other values too)`; (c) names 0x25F80006 and 0x25F80004 and not 0x25F80002. The fix is in the closure: keep every operation of the turn in `selfOps` (an earlier one is not dropped), carry `selfOps` through a merge that cannot close them (or close them against the values the loop head holds), and close over the constants even when the merge is `others`. P3, as A5-60: a loop that moves a register pointer between partitions is rare, and neither corpus image has one; but (c) is a false note. P3, and arguably a question: a function that reads the frame buffer after a swap is rare, and the asynchronous swap makes the barrier by position the honest one, which the data flow lacks (A5-68's own reason). Expected: the FB read after the `VDP1_FBCR` write is kept (frame buffer volatile in a function that writes FBCR with FCM set), or a note on the write that says addresses of the frame buffer mean the other buffer after the frame end. | A5-82 | 5 | P3 | Oracle (triage (b), a generator false positive; severity P3, set down from the provisional P2): the generator's pointer checks (3b `cache-read-pointer-unsaid`, 3c `pointer-effect-unsaid`) reported right texts in six seeds of grammar 2 beyond the nightly (64293, 303290, 202350 with `SE_GENERATED_ALPHABET=specials`, 310654, 313937, 405136; none below 20,000). The output states what it can; the rules took a register that the access was not through for its pointer. Three mechanisms, each read from the shrunk output (the area 3 round 8 review put 310654 and 405136 down to the pointer being a callee's output; what the oracle took for the pointer there is the number stored, and the callee's output is only why the text cannot name the pointer): (1) the register address is a stored or returned value, not an address used: `func_0x06009034(param_1,param_2); *extraout_r7 = 0xffffff9d;` (310654), `*(xunknown4 *)(extraout_r6 + 8) = 0xffffffe6;` (405136), `return CONCAT44((int4)*param_2,0xffffffa5);` (313937): the number is an on-chip register's address, the oracle took it for the pointer's base, and the pointer is a callee's output register or a parameter (A5-58, accepted: a callee-returned pointer is not followed); (2) the assignment of `&REG` stands below the access, in no loop, so it cannot reach it: `*(xunknown4 *)(param_3 + 6) = 4; if (param_2 == 0) { param_3 = &VDP2_WCTLA; }` after a signed-bound table the engine leaves as an indirect call (`Could not recover jumptable ... Too many branches`, `Treating indirect jump as call`; `cmp/gt` or `cmp/ge` with `bt` lets the index -1 through), whose cases set the pointer register in code the text does not hold (303290: SCSP_DMEAL write; 202350: a cached read of 0x1DFE0064, `param_4 = &VDP2_WCTLB;` below); (3) seed 64293 (the area 2 round 7 review's finding, which the area 3 review did not reduce): the pointer of `uVar3 = puVar6[7];` is a merge of the callee's output (`puVar6 = extraout_r7;`, which the callee set to the literal 0x05F80066, so the read is of the cached 0x05F80074) and `&SCSP_SCILV2`, and `puVar6 = &VDP2_MPGHRB;` (the same literal) is assigned after the read, outside every loop: made after the read on every path, it was taken for the read's derivation; no distance test separates it (0x05F80066 is 14 bytes from the register read). No engine or host defect: each text states what it can, and no reader is led to take a cache read for an ordinary one by it (the callee-output pointer is A5-58). Fix (oracle only, `Sh2GeneratedTests.cpp`): `PointerPrintsFromHardware` follows an assignment only if it can reach the read (it is above it, or a `do`/`while`/`for` block or a backward `goto` holds both), and `NamesHardwareBase` takes a register or a number as the pointer only within 60 bytes (the longest displacement, `kPointerReach`) of the register the emulator reached, or a hardware address no register is named at where the run went; the rest is counted `cache.read.pointer.unrelated` / `pointer.effect.unrelated`. Known limit: a number that is a value and lies within 60 bytes of the register reached would still be taken for the pointer (313937's 0xffffffa5 is 87 bytes from 0xFFFFFFFC). Siblings: grammar 2 seeds 60,001 to 80,000 find no other pointer-rule seed (60538 is area 3's; 66914, 69133, 69493 and 77197 are area 3 classes, `return-dropped:partial` and `kept-read:gbr`, not filed here). | `SE_GENERATED_GRAMMAR=2 SE_GENERATED_FIRST=<seed> SE_GENERATED_SEEDS=1` for each seed; pins in `TestA582Pins` (`Sh2GeneratedTests.cpp`): the six shrunk modules (no finding, and the derivation counted unrelated), the grammar 2 seeds 64293, 303290, 310654, 313937 and 405136 as programs, grammar 1 seeds 69 and 179 as controls (a register merged with a hardware address no register is named at stays the pointer), and nine derivations on text (assignment above, below, in a do-while, before a backward goto, in a loop not holding the read; 60 and 68 bytes; a stored value; unmapped hardware). 64293 shrunk: `SE_GENERATED_WORDS="2FD6 6013 6DF6 000B 0009 2F86 2F96 2FA6 2FE6 4F22 7FF0 6EF3 6843 6953 6A63 B02D 0009 6483 6593 66A3 E105 3616 8916 4608 C701 016E 412B 0009 0600 9050 0600 9050 0600 9054 0600 905C 0600 9058 0600 9058 A005 0009 A003 0009 A001 0009 D705 D106 7E10 6FE3 4F26 6EF6 6AF6 69F6 68F6 412B 0009 0009 25B0 0428 0600 907C 7FE8 E103 3616 8913 4608 C702 016E 412B 0009 0009 0600 90A0 0600 90A4 0600 90A8 0600 90AC A006 0009 A004 0009 A002 0009 8577 D702 E07D 7F18 000B 0009 05F8 0066" SE_GENERATED_FUNCS="0/0/a,a/a/72,7c/7c/b8" SE_GENERATED_TABLES="34:6:6:5c:50,50,54,5c,58,58;8a:6:4:ac:a0,a4,a8,ac" SE_GENERATED_RETURNS="1,1,1" SE_GENERATED_CLOBBERS="0,121000,101000" SE_GENERATED_READS="20000,40000,800"`; the other five shrunk replays are the pins' words | fixed c369e97 | area 2 round 7 review (seed 64293, routed to area 5, filed P2 provisional) and area 3 round 8 review (the same class, six seeds; merged) | area 5 A5-82 fix |

| A6-43 | 6 | P3 | A write that fails for want of room, met by an allocation failure while its error text is made, leaks the open file and leaves the staging directory. `WriteAndClose` (`FileWrite.cpp`) builds its message first (`error = Reason("could not write", path)`: a handful of strings) and calls `std::fclose(f)` after it: if one of those allocations throws, the `FILE*` and its descriptor are never closed, `WriteFileAtomically`'s `RemoveFile(temp)` never runs, and the `.separt-<pid>-<n>` temporary stays in the staging directory. In the `fclose` branch (`could not finish writing`) the descriptor is closed but the temporary stays the same way. `Extract`'s guard (A6-37) then unlinks its known paths, `rmdir` fails on the temporary, and it falls back to the allocating `RemoveEntry`: with one failed allocation that works (the directory goes; the FILE and descriptor stay), with an allocation failure that stays (sustained exhaustion) it cannot, and `<config>/decompiler/<hash>.tmp-<pid>` stays on disk (the three small files, the temporary, and the partial `sh-2.sla`); no later process removes another pid's. So A6-33 / A6-37's "removed on every exit but the move" holds for allocation failures only, which is all the matrix models: no family ever fails a write, `cold` never meets ENOSPC. A disk that is full AND an allocation that fails in a short string is rare, and the next request retries (A6-40) and succeeds, so P3: one `FILE` and a descriptor, or up to about 19 KB on disk, per event. Real ENOSPC alone is handled (a 24 KB tmpfs: Unavailable with the path, nothing left, Ok after `mount -o remount,size=1m`, the same process). | `<scratch> --diskfull [sticky]` (a hand mode of `SaturnExplorerDecompilerFaultTests`, `DiskFullProbe`): the first request of a process with `RLIMIT_FSIZE` at 5,000 bytes (the three small spec files fit, the 13 KB `sh-2.sla` does not: "directory made, three files written, the disk full on the fourth"; `SE_DF_LIMIT=<bytes>` moves it) and allocation k failing, for every k of that request (93 cells); then the limit is lifted and the next request must be Ok with no `.tmp-` entry in `<cfg>/decompiler` and no more descriptors than before. Plain: k = 67..70 fail "no descriptor is left open -- 4 -> 5" (a leaked `FILE` is also an LSan report under ASan). `sticky`: the same four fail that and "no staging directory is left -- 791f3d582826f873.tmp-<pid>". `SE_DF_LIMIT=100`, `1500`, `1600` with `sticky`: 5 cells of 73 / 87 / 87 each leave the staging directory (the `fclose` branch); their plain runs pass. Oracle: descriptor count and the staging listing. Expected: no cell fails; `WriteAndClose` closes the file before it makes any text, and the staging temporary is removed (or its name kept where the guard can unlink it) without an allocation | fixed 4fb39bf: `WriteAndClose` closes the file before it makes any text (errno captured and put back), `WriteFileAtomically` removes its staging file through a guard that allocates nothing, `OpenStagingFile` and `CreateStagingFile` release the descriptor and the name before their texts, and `Extract` leaves its failure paths to its non-allocating guard; jobs `diskfull plain 5000`, `diskfull sticky 5000`, `diskfull sticky 1500` | area 6 round 6 | area 6 round 6 fix |

The engine reads the `.pspec` and `.cspec` of the directory for every Architecture, so with the directory removed each request is `Failed` with "XML error parsing processor specification: Unable to open xml document" (no path, no hint; with one file damaged, "XML error parsing compiler specification: <path>/superh.cspec syntax..."), a Disassemble the same as one line, and the engine is not marked damaged. | A6-45 | 6 | P3 | An allocation failure in host code after the engine has finished marks the engine damaged for the rest of the process. `Sh2Decompiler::Decompile` treats any exception with `mWorkPhase != Idle` as "inside the engine" (`gEngineDamaged = true`, every later request and listing Unavailable: "the decompiler ran out of memory earlier and its engine may be damaged; restart Saturn Explorer"), and `mWorkPhase` is reset only when `Decompile` returns, so it still names the last phase after every Architecture is gone. Three stretches of pure host work follow that: the end of `DecompileInto` (`r.aliasedMemory`, `r.callees`, `r.calledAt` and its `std::sort`: the last two allocations of fixture1), `NoteRemovedAccesses` before its reference run starts (`DecompileRequest ref = request;` copies the whole request, names map included, which for an imported symbol file is the largest allocation of the request; `VolatileAliases`; the warning scan: 31 allocations of mailbox) and after it (39). In none of them is a translator, an Architecture or an engine object live, so nothing is half-reset (a failure in Init or Flow, where `Sleigh::reset` or an instruction decode runs, is what the marking is for); one out-of-memory in a `std::vector` here turns a transient condition into a decompiler that is gone until the user restarts, while the same allocation made before the first phase entry (the request's own preparation) is transient. No wrong result, no crash, and the user is told, so P3 (availability). The matrix encodes the behaviour: a failure after the first phase entry is `inEngine` and the next request must be Unavailable. | `<scratch> --cell alloc fixture1 24424` (and `24423`): trace `DecompileInto` / `std::vector<unsigned>::_M_realloc_insert` (`r.calledAt`), class `failed-in-engine`, `next=4` (Unavailable); `--cell alloc mailbox 23066`: the trace is `DecompileRequest::DecompileRequest(const&)` in `NoteRemovedAccesses`, `next=4`; `--cell alloc mailbox 36000` / `35980`: the reference run's tail, `next=4`. Expected: Failed("out of memory") for that request and `next=0` (Ok), engine not marked: `mWorkPhase` back to Idle once the last Architecture of the request is gone (and NoteRemovedAccesses' copy made before anything is entered), and the cell's `inEngine` taken from a flag the host clears there rather than from `engineEntered` | fixed 4fb39bf: `DecompileInto` sets `mWorkPhase` back to Idle when the last pass's Architecture is gone (after the boundary check that names the phase a cancel is honoured in), so the host tail, the request copy and the reference run's tail fail only their request; test hook `SetEngineDoneHookForTests`; the matrix's `inEngine` is cleared there and the alloc cells in the host-only stretches of the baseline log are pinned (`failed-after-engine`, `HOSTTAIL`) | area 6 round 6 | area 6 round 6 fix |
| A6-46 | 6 | P3 | `TestBaselines` in `DecompilerRunnerTests.cpp` hands the runner a phase observer that writes, by reference, to a vector and a mutex local to the function. No warning. Patch 0050's p-code is right (the decode oracle: 0 differ) and the text is the engine's: the spec makes `t * (target + 2) + (1 - t) * (slot + 4)`, `& 0xfffffffc`, `+ 4 * disp`, `RuleCollectTerms` / `Funcdata::distributeIntMultAdd` rewrite the sum into `t * 6 + 0x6009008` by changing the inputs of an existing INT_ADD in place, whose output keeps the non-zero mask of the value it used to be (`(1 - t) * 0x6009008`: 0xfffffffc, found by printing the tree at `RuleAndMask::applyOp`: `+ nz=fffffffc local=ffffffff`), and `RuleAndMask` takes the AND with 0xfffffffc for redundant. | A2-48 | 2 | P2 | A delayed branch translated twice in a row paints its slot's context on the instruction after the slot. `Sleigh::oneInstruction` applies the commits of the branch's parse tree (`pos->applyCommits()`, which evaluates `globalset(inst_next, ...)` from the tree's next address) and then moves that next address to the end of the delay slot (`pos->setNaddr(...+ fallOffset)`); when the second translation finds the tree in the cache (the ring of two of A2-5, LRU since patch 0020) the commits are applied again with `inst_next` now the address after the slot, so the slot's `slot_pc_valid` / `slot_tgt` (bra, bsr: patch 0005) or `slot_kind` / `slot_rm` (every other kind: patch 0050) are set on the instruction that follows it instead. The decode oracle cannot see it (its cells hold a nop after the slot and translate each branch once: `translation history` 0 of 5,632 differ). Product reach: none found. The emulator translates every instruction it executes (`EmulatePcodeCache::createInstruction` keeps no cache), so a tight loop of one delayed branch and its slot (`L: bf/s L ; dt r2`) poisons the instruction after the loop, but the exit of a bt/s or bf/s loop is the not-taken path, where the polluted p-code computes the ordinary PC from T; `ReturnCheck::Step` translates every instruction again and a branch twice in a row is rare. Experiment, not a fix: `pos->setNaddr(pos->getAddr() + pos->getLength())` before `applyCommits()` in `oneInstruction` makes the count 0, with the rest of the oracle unchanged. | `Sh2DecodeTests` `RunFollowers`: 60 pairs after one translation of the branch (must be 0, is 0), after two in a row (now `== 0`; reverting patch 0047 makes it 215 of 240); `SE_DECODE_FORM=<name>` selects the pairs | fixed ad80a00 (patch 0047: `Sleigh::oneInstruction` sets the tree's next address to the end of the branch itself before applying the commits, then to the end of the slot as before) | area 2 round 7 | area 2 round 7 fix |

The `returns` / `intent` flags need re-validating against a run (r0 written on every path by an instruction still in the function) after each shrink step, or the finding classes that read them (`return-dropped`, `return-dropped:partial`) should shrink only items that cannot change the fact. | seed 62956 grammar 2 | fixed ad80a00 (the shrinker refuses a step that leaves `return g(p);` without its call, `Shrinker::IntentHolds`, and `return-dropped` needs r0 written on every finished run; seed 62956 shrunk with the exclusion off keeps its call and compare and is a counterexample; pins `TestRound7Pins`) | area 2 round 7 | area 2 round 7 fix |
| A2-51 | 2 | P3 | (Oracle.) The decode oracle fails at other seeds than its default with cases it "could not run": at `SE_DECODE_SEED=555`, 2 million cases of each, jsr 8, jmp 4, bsrf 6 (and one jsr at seed 31337, 100,000 per form), "Could not obtain cached delay slot instruction" while translating an odd address. `PrepMem` takes the `@Rn` of jmp and jsr for a memory operand of width 1 (`OpDef::mem` is 0 for them) and overwrote the jump register `PickTarget` had just set with any address, odd ones included, and the slot's own `PrepMem` (or, since A2-46, its `RelateOperands`) can do the same to a branch register of any kind. An odd target is an address error on the hardware (A2-8), and the emulator translates code at an odd address, the middle of two instructions of the image: a slot-illegal word there fails the translation. The default seed and the CI runs never meet it; a reviewer's other seed does. The register's value (even, near the code or at the ends of the space) is what the case was meant to test, and the old override replaced it for jmp and jsr in most cases. | `SE_DECODE_FORM="jsr @Rn" SE_DECODE_CASES=2000000 SE_DECODE_SEED=555` on the base: 8 could not run | fixed 0cc677a (oracle only: the branch register is restored after both `PrepMem` calls and the widening; the failing example lines now carry the slot, the address being translated and the registers) | area 2 round 7 | area 2 round 7 |

| A2-52 | 2 | P1 | A2-47 is fixed for the op a rule rewrites and not for the partial results downstream of it, so the same wrong value comes out of ordinary arithmetic: a sum of scaled terms in which two terms share a variable, read through a mask, is printed without the mask. `c + 8 * x + 4 * y + 14 * x` followed by `shlr2 ; shll2` (a mask of `-4`) prints `return param_1 * 0x16 + 0x100 + param_3 * 4;` and the code returns `0x114` for r4 = 1, r6 = 0 where the text gives `0x116`. No warning. Cause, from the tree at the point of the rewrite (the checker below printed it): `RuleCollectTerms` combines `8 * x` and `14 * x` by changing the inputs of two existing ADDs in place: the one that held `8 * x` is made `c + 0` (`opSetInput`, zero coefficient), the other gets `22 * x`. Patch 0046 resets the non-zero mask of the OUTPUT of each op whose input `opSetInput` replaces, which is those two. The ADD between them, `(c + 8 * x) + 4 * y`, has no input replaced, but the value of one of its inputs changed (a multiple of 8 is now a multiple of 2) and it keeps the mask `0xfffffffc` that was true of `c + 8x + 4y`; `RuleAndMask`, which trusts `getNZMask()`, reads it and removes the `& 0xfffffffc` as redundant. Patch 0046 does not change this fixture (reverting it prints the same text): it covers a rule whose changed op is the one the mask is read from, and the value of every partial result below a changed op changes with it. The generator cannot draw it: its register grammar has no sum of products of one variable (8,072 printed-C functions with patch 0046 reverted, 6,894 compared, 0 findings; the A2-47 slot fixtures were the only reach). Found with a scratch invariant checker (`tools/a2_round8/nzm_check.patch`: after every rule application and every action, evaluate the function's ops on 8 assignments of the inputs and report an op whose value has a bit its non-zero mask says is clear, with the rule that left it so): the template `termgen` (sums of 2 to 4 terms `v * c` over r4-r6 and booleans, c from the literal pool, then a mask, a shift pair or an extension) finds 21 wrong values and 175 stale-mask reports in 6,000 seeds, 156 at `rule:collect_terms` and the rest downstream of it (8 wrong in 400 with patch 0046 reverted, 2 in 400 with it). Images 007 and 008: the checker reports no stale mask in any of their functions of at most 400 ops (839 and 1,554 functions), so no corpus function is a known victim. | A2-53 | 2 | P3 | The stack pointer on entry is taken to be 256-aligned, so a value computed from it is folded as if its low byte were zero: `mov r15,r0 ; and #0xff,r0 ; rts ; nop` is printed `return 0;` and the code returns the low byte of r15, whatever the caller left in it (`add #-5,r15 ; mov r15,r0 ; and #0x1c,r0` is printed `return 0x18;`; rounding a frame address up to 8, `mov r15,r0 ; add #7,r0 ; mov #-8,r1 ; and r1,r0`, is printed `return &xStack00000000;`, the frame address itself, where a stack pointer that is 4 mod 8 gives r15 + 4). Cause: `Funcdata::calcNZMask` (`funcdata_varnode.cc`, upstream's) gives a spacebase input the mask `~0xff` ("treat spacebase input as aligned"), and `RuleAndMask` and the rules that read masks take it as a fact. SH-2 code keeps r15 4-aligned, and code that rounds a frame (`mov r15,r1 ; add #7,r1 ; mov #-8,r2 ; and r2,r1`) or tests the pointer's alignment reads exactly the bits this removes. The run cannot tell: the call oracle's r15 is `0x0600F000`, 256-aligned, and the generator's stack sits at an aligned base. I did not look for such a function in the corpus images. A function that depends on r15's low byte is the exception, hence P3, but the text is wrong with no warning when it does. | `CallFixture { 0x06009000, { 0x60F3, 0xC9FF, 0x000B, 0x0009 } }` (and `{ 0x7FFB, 0x60F3, 0xC91C, 0x7F05, 0x000B, 0x0009 }`, `{ 0x60F3, 0x7007, 0xE1F8, 0x2019, 0x000B, 0x0009 }`): the text is `return 0;` (`return 0x18;`, `return &xStack00000000;`); the code with r15 = 0x0600F004 or any value not a multiple of 256 returns `r15 & 0xff` (`(r15 - 5) & 0x1c`, `r15 + 4`). A 4-aligned r15 makes `& 3` and the offset alone right, so those are no example. | A2-58 | 2 | P1 | Patch 0051 is the class for `totalReplace` and not for every route by which a Varnode that already exists gains a reader: `Funcdata::opSetInput` leaves the consume mask of the new input as it was, and the next `ActionVarnodeProps` replaces a Varnode whose non-zero mask and consume mask do not meet by a zero. `RulePushMulti` is one such route (`ruleaction.cc`, `data.opSetInput(op1,substitute->getOut(),slot1)` after `findSubstitute`): two functionally equal computations in the two predecessors of a join (an add, a test, a shift) are merged into one op in the join, and that op now reads the join's existing MULTIEQUAL, whose consume mask was worked out from the readers it had before. Printed with no warning, and the signature loses the inputs (`xunknown4 func_0x06009000(void)`): `mov #1,r3 ; rotr r3 ; cmp/pl r4 ; bt B ; A: mov r5,r1 ; and r3,r1 ; mov r1,r2 ; add #1,r2 ; bra J ; nop ; B: mov r6,r1 ; and r3,r1 ; mov r1,r2 ; add #1,r2 ; J: mov r1,r0 ; extu.b r0,r0 ; or r2,r0 ; rts ; nop` returns `r1 + 1`, r1 being 0 or 0x80000000, and the text is `return 1;` (r4 = 0, r5 = 0x80000001: the code returns 0x80000001). The same shape with the test, `tst r1,r1` in both arms and `shll2 r0 ; movt r2 ; sub r2,r0` at the join, returns 0 when the chosen source has its sign bit set and -1 when it has not, and the text is `return 0xffffffff;` (r4 = 2, r6 = 0xFFFF8000: the code returns 0). Cause, from the per-step dump of the instrumented engine (`SE_DUMP_EACH=1 SE_DUMP_CONSUME=1 SE_DUMP_FN=...`): before `rule:push_multi` the join phi `r1 = r1(A) ? r1(B)` has `consume=ff` (its readers were the byte read `extu.b` and a copy); the rule re-points the add at that phi (`r2 = r1 + 1` now reads it), which keeps `consume=ff`; a second `rule:push_multi` hoists the AND (`r1 = (r5 ? r6) & 0x80000000`), `rule:andorlump` folds the byte read to `x & 0`, and `rule:andmask` replaces `r1 = x & 0x80000000` by `#0` in the same pass: `RuleAndMask`'s branch `(andmask & out->getConsume()) == 0` (andmask `0x80000000`, consume `0xff`). The next `ActionVarnodeProps` would do the same; the rule gets there first. Found with the consume checker of `tools/a2_round9/` (`nzm_check_r9.patch`, which also evaluates through MULTIEQUAL, LOAD and call outputs: the round 8 checker could not, and so checked little of the corpus), on the generator family `join` (`tools/a2_round9/dagen.py` families 7, 8 and 9): 9 of 600, 102 of 600 and 43 of 600 functions are printed wrong on the head, every one of the 43 of family 9 reported by the checker at `rule:push_multi`; none in the nightly's grammars, which draw no join whose predecessors compute equal ops that the join reads narrowly and wholly (grammar 2 at 900,001 to 920,000, grammar 1 at 900,001 to 910,000, printed-C at 980,001 to 995,000: no finding of this area; the two failing seeds of grammar 2 are other areas', routed in the record). | A2-59 | 2 | P2 | The engine does not finish a counted loop that ANDs a constant-seeded accumulator with a value assembled from the pieces of a register, and its memory grows without bound until the host cancels. `mov #4,r1 ; L: swap.b r2,r3 ; and r3,r1 ; dt r6 ; bf L ; rts ; mov r1,r0` (`E104 6328 2139 4610 8BFB 000B 6013 0009`) never returns from `Decompile`: 100 % of one core and about 44 MB a second (RSS 368 MB after 8 s, 1.4 GB after 32 s), the host's cancel flag is the only stop (`ActionPool::apply` checks it every 1024 ops; nothing bounds the number of main-loop passes). It is a regression of patch 0049: with the walk of `opSetInput` reverted (0046 kept) the same words finish, and so do the 161 `loopand` functions I tried (of the 686 that hang on the head). Cause, from the per-step dump (`SE_DUMP_EACH=1`): every pass of the main loop applies `rule:anddistribute` and then `rule:humptyor`, which undo each other (`(A \| B) & C => (A & C) \| (B & C)` and `(V & W) \| (V & X) => V & (W \| X)`), 3,504 dumps in 3 s, `ActionHeritage` passes counting up (447,292 to 577,506 in a few seconds). `swap.b` is the OR of three disjoint pieces of r2 (`r2 & 0xffff0000`, `(r2 & 0xff) << 8`, `(r2 & 0xff00) >> 8`); r1 is `4` or `r1 & r3`, so its non-zero mask is `0x4`, and `RuleAndDistribute` distributes the AND over the OR when a piece's non-zero mask does not meet it, then the next distribution has to take the inner OR in the same pass. Without 0049 two `anddistribute` fire in a row and `andmask` cancels the pieces (`rule:anddistribute rule:anddistribute rule:andmask`); with it the first `opSetInput` has forgotten the mask of the phi `r1` and everything below it, `RuleAndDistribute` takes `othermask == fullmask` for 'nothing useful' and does not fire the second time, `RuleHumptyOr` sees `(A & r1) \| (C & r1)` and merges it back, `ActionNonzeroMask` works the masks out again at the next pass, and the cycle repeats. Needs a loop (a phi that carries the narrow mask); the same words without the loop, with `or` or `xor` for the AND, or with a full mask (`E1FF`) finish. The generators did not see it: grammar 2 at 900,001 to 920,000, grammar 1 at 900,001 to 910,000 and printed-C at 980,001 to 995,000 (45,000 functions) have no loop that carries an AND of a constant-seeded accumulator with a piece-assembled operand. Frequency: 686 of 5,500 `loopand` functions (12.5 %, `dagen.py` family 11: 194 of seeds 1 to 1,500 and 492 of 20,001 to 24,000), 2 of 9,000 of the general loop family (family 3: seeds 10,001 to 13,000 and 30,001 to 36,000; `E601 E17F 222B 200D 2109 4610 8BFA 4308 6023 000B 0009 0009` is one of the two); the 2,393 functions of the two game images do not contain one. Other rule pairs that undo each other under a mask guard were not searched for. | `SE_GENERATED_PRINTEDC=only SE_GENERATED_PRINTEDC_WORDS="E104 6328 2139 4610 8BFB 000B 6013 0009"` with `timeout 10` (exit 124; do not run it without the timeout). More: `E104 6329 2139 4610 8BFB 000B 6013 0009` (`swap.w`), `E11C E601 6323 4319 6338 2139 4610 8BF9 000B 6013 000B 6033`, `E601 E17F 222B 200D 2109 4610 8BFA 4308 6023 000B 0009 0009` (`xtrct`, in `tools/a2_round9/fixtures.txt` as comments: the fixture runner would not return). Shrinking: `tools/a2_round9/ddmin_hang.py`; scanning: `hangscan.sh`. Expected: a text (`do { ... } while (...)` returning the accumulator ANDed with the swapped value) in the time it takes for the loop without the swap, and a bound on the passes of the main loop so that an oscillation between two rules ends as an error and not as an exhaustion of memory. Not pinned: a pin that hangs cannot run in `ctest`; the words are the pin for the fix's author. | fixed 858f287 (patch 0054: the changed op's output gets the mask its inputs give and nothing true is forgotten, so the distributed AND leaves the phi its `0x4` and the second `RuleAndDistribute` fires; patch 0055: a repeating group or pool stops after 300 passes with `Exceeded maximum repeats of <action> with changes still pending`, the host withholds the signature ('the analysis did not settle (repeat limit)') and the generator reports `unsettled`. The engine's reading of a text in the printed-C differential (`CxxHeader`) drops the `U` suffix of an unsigned literal, so `param_1 + 0x80U` of a signed `param_1` is a signed sum and `0x7f < param_1 + 0x80U` a signed compare, where C and the engine mean an unsigned one (`Promoted<N, S>` brings a plain number to the other operand's type, signed or not). A right text that the C reading cannot take for another reason (here `(int4)-param_2` of a two-byte `param_2`, which C widens first) is then `mismatch` in both readings and a finding: `6043 6153 6263 6373 4404 4421 323C 651B 4421 D104 301C 3012 0129 321C 6053 600F 000B 0009 0000 0080` prints `CONCAT44((int4)-param_2,(uint4)(0x7f < param_1 + 0x80U))`, returns r0 = `0xFFFF8000` and r1 = 0 for r4 = 0xFFFFFFFF, r5 = 0x8000: the C reading gets the high word wrong (`0x00008000`: C widens the two-byte `param_2` before negating), the engine's reading the low word (for r4 = 0x7FFFFFFF the sum `0x8000007F` is above 0x7f as an unsigned number, the reading makes it a negative signed one and the compare false). With `0x80U` written `uint4(0x80)` in the kept C++ file (`SE_GENERATED_KEEP=1`, `printedc_0_engine.cpp`) the engine's reading agrees on every input. The brief's question, whether `and #3` printing 0 is right for every SH-2 entry: it is for every entry whose r15 is a multiple of 4, which compiled code keeps (the SH-2 faults on a longword push through any other), and it is not for hand-written code that calls with a word pushed. | `SE_AREA2_FIXTURES=tools/a2_round9/fixtures.txt`: `A2-62 sp assumed aligned` (`60F3 7002 C903 000B 0009`, text `return 2;`; the run, r15 = 0x0600F000, agrees). | A3-56 | 3 | P2 | A3-55's rule leaves out r0's own entry value as a chooser: `ReturnCheck` records a branch condition with `s.ctl \|= cond & kNotParameters & ~1u`, and the `& ~1u` drops r0 on the ground that r0's entry value is A3-14's. It is A3-14's when r0's entry value is an alternative of the join or the value is computed from it (the `dt r0` loop and the `and r0,r0` function that A3-45 and A3-46 keep lent). Here neither holds: both alternatives are written by the function and the hidden r0 only chooses between them. `tst r0,r0 ; bt L ; mov #1,r0 ; or r0,r1 ; bra M ; nop ; L: mov #2,r0 ; or r0,r1 ; M: rts ; nop` is lent `void f(void)` with 0 parameters and printed `return;`: the code returns 2 for r0 = 0 and 1 otherwise, and a caller's setup of r0 is dead in its text.
