A copy of r15 walked down by pre-decrement stores is turned by the engine into `register0x3c` (`BADSPACEBASE`), so its stores leave the stack space and the local they overwrite is folded to its first value. Leaf, no call, lent: `add #-8,r15 ; mov #5,r0 ; mov.l r0,@r15 ; mov r15,r2 ; add #8,r2 ; mov #2,r1 ; L: mov.l r1,@-r2 ; dt r1 ; bf L ; mov.l @r15,r0 ; rts ; add #8,r15` prints `return 5;`, the code returns 1. A walk upward is right; a host alias changes nothing. `*--p = v` over a local array compiles to this shape. Found from generator grammar 2 seed 901303 (`n:kept-read:r13`). Cause (the fix's record, "A3-65 and A5-85"): every part of upstream's stack analysis takes a pointer into the frame to reach only what lies at or above its base, as an index does. `AliasChecker` set the alias boundary at the walk's start, so the locals below it were unaliased and the INDIRECTs the walk's stores made for them were collapsed; the guard of the indexed store (`LoadGuard::establishRange`) covered the three iterations the partial value-set analysis ran, or what lies above the base; a walk from the entry r15 itself merged the register into the loop's pointer (`register0x0000003c`); and a register's save slot, storage no local is mapped to, was one variable with the register, so code that runs on after its pops (901303) read the walk's byte as the saved r13. Fixed by engine patch 0056 (`0056-engine-stack-walk-down-aliases.patch`): a base a loop steps down aliases everything from 0xffff below it up, the guard covers the stack below a pointer stepped down (and no array hint is made at that guessed bottom), the join of the entry r15 gets a `PTRSUB` (and is no reference to a stack parameter), a save slot a walk down reaches stays mapped. Check 12 takes a save into a stack local before any call (`xStack_c = unaff_r13;`) for no read of the register. | A3-66 | 3 | P2 | The return rule's "a value nothing is known of is taken for memory" (`ReturnInput`) drops every input that value is joined or combined with. A value is "nothing known" when every register is among its dependences (`(d & kRegisters) == kRegisters`), and `Merge`, `Sum` and `OpDeps` union dependences, so `phi(r3, nothing-known)` and `nothing-known + r3` are nothing known as a whole and `ReturnInput` returns 0 for them: r3 leaves the data. The exception was made for a load through a pointer that may be anywhere in the stack (06038F64 and 06038F72 of image 008), which reads memory; it was written for the value, not for the values it is combined with. The engine drops the return of the join of a copy of an unheld input with anything (`AncestorRealistic`: a failing branch of a MULTIEQUAL fails the join) and of any value that has a second use, so the input leaves the text and the signature with it. The nothing-known value is easy to reach by hand: a load below r15 (`mov r15,r1 ; add #-4,r1 ; mov.l @r1,r0`), and, with no unusual code at all, **any stack access whose offset is a literal-pool word**: `WithholdReason`'s `ReturnCheck` (unlike the probe, which has `SetLiterals`) does not fold a pool load, so `mov.w =20,r2 ; add r15,r2 ; mov.l @r2,r0` is the frame plus a loaded index, a stack address that is nothing known, and a load through it gives `kAnything`. A3-62's stack argument as input is invisible through it (the same read with `mov r15,r2 ; add #20,r2` is withheld, "computed from a stack argument"). Hand-written only for the first shapes (compiled code reads no input it does not hold); P2 as A3-55 to A3-62: a signature that drops an input and a text that shows no trace of it. | A3-74 | 3 | P3 | The cost of A3-61's callee walk is bounded by steps and not by what a step costs. `kNestedMaxSteps` (20,000) counts instruction visits, and each visit copies a `State` whose `slots` map grows with every distinct stack word the callee stores to, `Store` and `Clobber` walk all slots, and `in` keeps a state per address: time and memory are quadratic in the callee's stores, and the 20,000 steps allow a callee of 20,000 pushes (a 40 KB function). Nothing in `Run` checks the cancel flag (A6's cancel at every thousand ops does not reach it). The walk is not recursive (`mNested` asks no further, which is why it is bounded in depth) and is asked once per target per probe, but a probe is run for every function that has a call and reads back a word it wrote before it: a restoring `lds.l @r15+,pr` after the call is such a read (`ReadExposed`), so every ordinary caller of the callee walks it, in every decompile of every caller. The same caller that keeps PR in r8 and reads no stack word takes 5 ms. The corpus's 10 to 12 % is the common case (many small callees); the cost is bad only for an adversarial or machine-generated callee, and the main `ReturnCheck` of a function with that many pushes has the same bound (200,000 steps) as before. | A2-66 | 2 | P3 | (Instrument, closed here.) What patches 0054 and 0055 were attacked with this round, and what it found. (1) `tools/a2_round10/apply_struct.py`, on top of `a2_round9/nzm_check_r9.patch`, adds a structural checker: after every rule that fires and every leaf action that changes something, for every complete op, the non-zero mask of its output must hold `getNZMaskLocal(false)` of its inputs' masks (kind N) and the consume mask of each input must hold `Funcdata::inputConsumeNeeded` (kind C), a violation being reported against the first step it is seen at; after every `ActionDeadCode` pass the consume masks are compared with the model's least fixpoint of the roots and local steps (kind X). 11.2 million steps over 100,000 functions (families 0 to 19, seeds 100,001 to 105,000) and 3.9 million over the two game images, no limit on a function's size: no kind N; kind C only on a LOAD's output (the hold that `ActionDeadCode::lastChanceLoad` sets after its pass, so the cached mask is the narrower one of two true ones for a value whose readers decide) and on three BRANCHIND indexes (the model takes every bit, `jt->getSwitchVarConsume()` is narrower: the cached is the wider, which is allowed); kind X (111 and 341 in the images) is masks wider than the model, for roots the model does not have (a CALLOTHER output's `holdOutput`, the COPY of a stack store that an INDIRECT names, the outputs of DELAY_SLOT and BUILD): the safe direction. (2) A tight-mask stress, the opposite of the round 9 fixer's forget-everything stress (`SE_EXACT_STRESS`: after every rule the non-zero masks made exact, mode 2, or the consume masks made the model's, mode 3): mode 2 over 19,000 functions (families 0 to 18) with the bound at 60: no hang, no bound hit, no finding but A2-65's. Mode 3 prints wrong values in stack-heavy functions because its model has no roots for the states between the return recovery's trials; it was not pursued (an instrument limit: kind X is empty on the same functions after every real pass). (3) `dagen10.py` families 12 to 19: ADD and SUB, OR and XOR with two accumulators, mixed, nested, conditional, masked and CRC-shaped accumulators in loops, two returns, T producers with a literal. Hang scans in batches of 100 with a 120 s limit over 30,000 loop functions: none (five batches that timed out were my own pauses; none took 20 s alone); 0 bound warnings anywhere. Family 19 found A2-64. | `Docs/Decompiler/tools/a2_round10/`: `dagen10.py`, `apply_struct.py` (with `structcheck_action_cc.txt`, `structcheck_funcdata_op.cc.txt`, `exactstress_action_cc.txt`), `fam_struct.sh`, `fam_exact.sh`, `rehang.sh`, `gen_range.sh`, `decode_seeds.sh`, `run_family.sh`, `hangscan.sh`, `fixtures.txt`. | A2-68 | 2 | P3 | `CircleRange::intersect` answers an empty set for two ranges that have common elements when one has a step and a bound of the other, rounded to the step, leaves the mask: `newStride` rounds both bounds of the step-1 range to the new phase and takes the range as empty when the order of the bounds changed (`origOrder != newOrder`), which is also what happens when a bound rounds past the top of the mask and wraps, or when a wrapped range's left bound rounds past it. `[0x39,1)` step 4 with `[0x73,0xff)` (a byte, the right bound 0xff rounds to 0x101, which is 1) is `(empty)` with answer 0, where 0x75, 0x79, ... 0xfd are in both; `[7,0xffff)` with `[0xa,0xfffe)` step 4 is empty (16,381 elements missing); `[0xfffffffe,0x80000000)` (`x s> -3`) with `[0,0x80000000)` step 4 (a value that is a multiple of 4) is empty, the left bound rounding from 0xfffffffe past 0xffffffff. Of 6,000 random pairs per width (seed 1), 169 of 3,553 with a step are wrong at 1 byte and 71 of 3,528 at 2 bytes; with both steps 1 (what `RuleRangeMeld` meets, ranges pulled back through compares) none of 3,940 is, and `circleUnion` is exact in all 3,859 pairs it answers. The users of `intersect` with a step are `JumpBasic::calcRange` (the guard of a switch variable that is known to be a multiple of 2^k, `getInitialRange`'s `rng.setRange(0, nzmask + step, ...)`, with `GuardRecord`'s range) and `CircleRange::pullBack(usenzmask)` (the guard range with the stride of the value's non-zero mask): a guard whose bound lies within one step of the top of the mask, or whose signed left bound is below zero and not a multiple of the step, makes the guard empty, which `analyzeGuards` takes as the end of the chain (`if (rng.isEmpty()) break;`) and `calcRange` as a range of size 0. That is reached with a scaled index and a signed lower bound of -1, -2 or -3 (`negguard11.py`: `mov r4,r0 ; shll2 r0 ; mov #-2,r1 ; cmp/ge r1,r0 ; bf default ; cmp/hi ...`): the guard is not folded into the switch and stays around it (`if (-3 < param_1 * 4) { switch(param_1 * 4) { ... } }`), where the bounds -4 to -8 fold (`switch(param_1 * 4)` alone). The text is right; the guard is only not recognised. Upstream's code, no local patch touches it. The caller that can meet two masks is `JumpBasic::calcRange` (a guard whose varnode has another size than the switch variable: `GuardRecord::valueMatch` accepts two quasi-copies of one base with the same preserved bits); I did not build a switch that does. | A2-82 | 2 | P2 | `RuleDivOpt` (upstream's, `ruleaction.cc`: `findForm`, `calcDivisor`, `applyOp`) turns `ext(x) * y >> n` into a division by a constant and prints a value the code does not compute, with no warning, in two ways. (a) `findForm`'s 'check for signed mismatch' takes a sign extension with a logical shift (`INT_SEXT` and `INT_RIGHT`) and a zero extension with an arithmetic one (`INT_ZEXT` and `INT_SRIGHT`) for a division whenever `8*outsize - n == xsize`, on the reasoning 'all the extension bits are truncated'; but the division it builds is as wide as the output and sign-filled where the shift filled its top n bits with zero (or with the sign), so the two differ in those n bits as soon as the product is negative (or, for the other pair, has its top bit set) and the result is read whole. `extsw r4,r1 ; mov.l #0x00010001,r2 ; mul.l r2,r1 ; sts macl,r0 ; shlr16 r0 ; rts` is `(unsigned)((short)x * 65537) >> 16` and prints `int4 func(int2 param_1) { return (int4)param_1 / 1 + ((int4)param_1 >> 0x1f); }`: for x = -1 the code returns 0x0000FFFE and the text -2; with `add r1,r0` after the shift, `iVar1 / 1 + (iVar1 >> 0x1f) + iVar1`, 0x0000FFFD against -3. (b) nothing in `findForm` or `calcDivisor` asks that the multiply not wrap: the product of the widest x and y must fit the width of the INT_MULT (2^xsize * y <= 2^(8*size), or the signed analogue), because the division is exact and the machine keeps the low bits; `extuw r4,r1 ; mov.l #0x01000001,r2 ; mul.l ; sts macl,r0 ; shlr16 ; shlr8 ; and #0xff,r0` is `(x * 0x01000001 >> 24) & 0xff`, which is `x & 0xff`, and prints `uint4 func(uint2 param_1) { return param_1 / 1; }` (x = 0xFFFF: the code 0xFF, the text 0xFFFF). The same two failures with the divisors 3, 6, 0x80, 0x200, ... of the table of magic numbers (`uint1 / 3` for `extub ; * 0x2AAAAAAB ; >> 31`). Found by `dagen13.py` family 60 `mulshift` (an extended byte or word times a constant, shifted logically or arithmetically, cut or whole: 27 of its first 1,500 functions are `mismatch`; family 56's new forms 6 and 7 the same) after the in-situ check of the rule (`rulecheck13.hh`) had flagged the sign-mismatched samples; the check's default skips the samples whose product wraps (RuleDivTermAdd leaves such a product on the way to RuleDivOpt), which is why (b) was first seen as a printed-C `mismatch`, and `SE_RULECHECK_NOWRAP=1` shows 94 bad applications of 260 in those 1,500 functions. Reproduces at the head 79906c2. Round 12 read `findForm` and `calcDivisor` against their documentation and called their arithmetic right where the engine can reach it, and no earlier family built a sign extension, a constant multiply at 32 bits and a logical shift together; the corpora do not contain it (the two images through the in-situ check without the wrap filter: 220 DivOpt applications, the only samples flagged are the `x / 7` fixture's widened product, which is DivTermAdd's, and the printed texts do not change). | (a) `SE_GENERATED_PRINTEDC=only SE_GENERATED_PRINTEDC_WORDS="614F D203 0127 001A 4029 000B 0009 0009 0001 0001"`: `mismatch (f0 r4=FFFFFFFF ... size=4 run=0000FFFE:FFFFFFFF pseudo=FFFFFFFE:00000000)`; the same with `"614F D203 0127 001A 4029 301C 000B 0009 0001 0001"` (`run=0000FFFD`, `pseudo=FFFFFFFD`) and `"614E D203 0127 001A 4029 4019 000B 0009 0000 8001"` (`(int4)param_1 / 0x200 + ((int4)param_1 >> 0x1f)` for a byte times 0x8001 shifted by 24: `run=000000FF`, `pseudo=FFFFFFFF`). (b) `"614D D204 0127 001A 4029 4019 C9FF 000B 0009 0009 0100 0001"`: `mismatch (... run=000000FF:0000FFFF pseudo=0000FFFF:00000000)`; `"614C D206 0127 001A 6303 6033 4029 4019 4009 4009 4009 4001 000B 0009 2AAA AAAB"`: `uint4 func(uint1 param_1) { return param_1 / 3; }`, `mismatch (... run=00000001:000000FF pseudo=00000055:00000000)`. The family: `python3 -I tools/a2_round13/dagen13.py 2350001 500 60` (13 of 500 `mismatch`; 2350501: 6; 2351001: 8), through `SE_GENERATED_PRINTEDC_WORDS`. Rule level: `rulefuzz13` `RF_WIDE=1 RF_CONSUME=1` seed 1059 (`SEXT(x:2) * 0x8001`, `INT_SRIGHT` by 15 at two bytes) and `RF_TMPL=1 RF_CONSUME=1` seed 34219. Suite (round 13 fix): `TestDivOptSignAndWrapRound13` (23,232 graphs, 2,518,272 values), the printed-C fixtures `A2-82 (a)` x4, `A2-82 (b)` x2 and `A2-82 control` in `Sh2GeneratedTests` (the row's five and one more, found by a search of constants for the zero extension with the arithmetic shift), `tools/a2_round13/replayfix13.sh` (seeds 1059 and 34219 of `rulefuzz13`; the third condition below). | fixed 038d038 (patch 0064: `findForm` takes a sign extension with a logical shift, or a zero extension with an arithmetic one, only if every reader of the result looks below the differing bits (a SUBPIECE from the bottom or an AND with a constant that fits; the consume mask cannot say it: it drops the bits the non-zero mask has as zero, which are the bits a logical shift fills), and a multiply only if its largest product fits the INT_MULT (the exact product `RuleDivTermAdd` and `RuleDivTermAdd2` build and flag excepted); a third condition, found while fixing: the divisor must fit the size the division is made at, signed ones not negative there (`sext(x:2) * 0xffff >> 31` is a division by 0x8001), and a shift amount over 127 is refused. | A2-85 | 2 | P3 | (Latent, upstream's, no SH-2 code reaches it.) Seven rules read a shift amount, a constant of any size, as an `int4` or as the count of a C++ shift: `RuleShiftCompare` (`INT_LEFT` by `sa` of the width or more: `x:2 << 0x1f == 0` builds the mask `(1 << (8*size - sa)) - 1` with a negative count, which is a shift by 49 on x86, and the compare becomes `x == 0` where it is always true), `RuleDoubleArithShift` (`(int4)c + (int4)d` of two amounts of 2^31 or more), `RuleSubNormal`, `RuleSubRight`, `RuleSubExtComm` (an amount of 2^31 or more taken as a negative `int4`), `RuleSubvarSext` and `RuleLzcountShiftBool` (`max_return >> shift` for a shift of 64 or more: `lzcount(x:2) >> 0x84` is 0, the rule makes it `x == 0`). The SH-2 spec's shift constants are at most 32 (`shll16`, the `<< 32` of `mac`), `rm & 0x1f` and the 64-bit forms, and `RuleDoubleShift` merges a chain of them into 0 at the width, so no amount of 2^31 or of the width reaches these rules; family 57 (`dagen13.py`, totals of 8 to 72 bits of left shifts, then a cut and a compare, a test or a shift back: 300 functions each run) applies `RuleShiftCompare` 26 times in situ and is right every time. | `RF_CONSUME=1 rulefuzz13 <scratch> 266405 1 3` (ShiftCompare), `410191` (LzcountShiftBool); `RF_WIDE=1 rulefuzz13 <scratch> 3949 1 3` (SubNormal), `5940` (DoubleArithShift), `793` (SubRight), `154892` (SubExtComm), `102166` (SubvarSext). 436 wrong applications in 800,000 `RF_WIDE=1` graphs; the wide mode draws amounts of 0x80000000, 0xffffffff and 0x100000000. Suite: `TestRuleGuardsRound13` (18 shift amounts from 1 to 2^63 at sizes 2 and 4 for `RuleShiftCompare`, `RuleDoubleArithShift`, `RuleSubNormal`, `RuleSubRight`, `RuleLzcountShiftBool`; `RuleSubExtComm` cuts up to 0xfffffffff0000002; `RuleSubvarSext` shifts of a sign-extended piece by 2^32 and others); `replayfix13.sh` (all seven seeds). | fixed 038d038 (patch 0064, each rule reads the 64-bit amount before it casts it: `RuleShiftCompare` and `RuleSubNormal` refuse a shift of the width or more, `RuleDoubleArithShift` saturates each amount at the width less one before adding, `RuleSubRight` adds in 64 bits, `RuleSubExtComm` refuses a cut outside the value, `RuleLzcountShiftBool` needs a shift under 64. `RuleSubvarSext`'s failure was the constant, not the cast: the sub-variable flow kept `x s>> 0x100000000` in a constant of the logical size, which holds 0; `traceForward` and `traceBackward` of `INT_SRIGHT` refuse an amount the size cannot hold. Reverting each fails the pin (56, 12, 18, 10, 6, 4 and 2 CHECKs in that order). The other `(int4)` reads of `subflow.cc` (`INT_LEFT`, `INT_RIGHT`, `SUBPIECE`) are left as upstream's: a mutant that saturates them lives through the pins and 1.8 million wide-amount graphs. What this round attacked area 2 with and what each found; all in `Docs/Decompiler/tools/a2_round13/` (the record is the section 'Area 2, round 13 review' of `15_DECOMPILER_STEP5.md`). (1) `rangeset13.cpp`: patch 0062's `newDomain`, `setNZMask` and `widen` against sets written out from the definition at every size from 1 to 8 bytes and every pair of sizes: 133.5 million membership checks over four seeds, 0 bad; against the pre-0062 `rangeutil.cc` it finds 4,147; the new pins `TestRangeSetsRound13` and `TestSignHighBitRound13` fail on that file and on the A2-81 revert. (2) `rulefuzz13.cpp`: a by-value fuzzer of every rule of the pool on random graphs, with templates of the shapes of the SH-2 spec and the engine's rewrites (shifts by the width and more, wide amounts, sign and zero extensions, consume masks): about 14 million graphs, finds the A2-80 and A2-81 reverts at once, `runmut13.sh` runs it against round 12's mutants (16 of them; 13 are killed in 300,000 graphs each, 11, 12 and 15 live); the defects are A2-82 to A2-87. (3) `rulecheck13.hh` with `mkrc13.sh` and `relink13.sh`: a hook in `ActionPool::processOp` that compares the value of the cone of the op, its readers and theirs before and after every rule application, in a scratch build: 69.8 million checks of 1.77 million applications over families 0 to 60 (300 functions each, family 23 excepted: too slow to run) and the two images without lending (31 and 102 million checks of 1.06 and 2.73 million applications, the first build of the check); it is blind where the engine is (a wrapped product is skipped by default, `SE_RULECHECK_NOWRAP`; the interior ADDs of a `RuleCollectTerms` tree, the zext `RuleBoolZext` negates, a root with consume 0 and a sample outside an output's own non-zero mask are not compared). (4) `dagen13.py` families 55 to 60 (the soup of shifts, rotates and multiplies; fixed-point blends; narrowed shifts; negated values shifted right; a shift then a division by a large constant; an extension times a constant, shifted). (5) `castmodel13.cpp` and `mkcast13.sh`: the printed-C oracle's casts, below. (6) `oraclevar13.sh`: the oracle with a special case of its second reading changed. The oracle (item 3 of the brief): the second reading (`CxxHeader`) reads `(uint8)x` of a signed 4-byte x as a zero extension (A2-19) and the cast of a 5 to 7 byte CONCAT piece as one (A2-79). Every printed cast of an extension, 8,798 prints of 45 families, has the type the printer's own test needs (a ZEXT cast with an unsigned or bool input, a SEXT cast with a signed input; no ZEXT of a signed input is printed as a cast or a function), the 5 to 7 byte pieces are all ZEXT of an unsigned 5 or 6 byte piece (124 prints, 0 of 7 bytes, none of 2, 3 or 4 bytes), and the one signed piece is a 2-byte SEXT, as the reading has it. The zero-extension reading of `(uint8)` over a signed 4-byte operand is load-bearing in 6 of 10,000 printed-C seeds and in the suite's fixtures A2-12 and A2-79 (the oracle with C's reading there turns them to `mismatch`): in all of them the engine's op is a ZEXT of a uint4 arithmetic result (`INT_ADD`, `INT_SUB`, `INT_AND`, `INT_XOR`) printed over an `int4` declaration, so the reading is the engine's meaning and the text is what A2-19 says it is. It cannot excuse a sign extension printed as `(uint8)`: the engine prints a SEXT with a signed output type `(int8)`, and a mutant that builds a SEXT where RulePiece2Zext builds a ZEXT gives 15 findings in 1,000 printed-C seeds. No misreading in either direction was found. | `tools/a2_round13/`: `rangeset13.cpp` (`build13.sh`), `rulefuzz13.cpp`, `rulecheck13.hh`, `mkrc13.sh`, `relink13.sh`, `famrc13.sh`, `dagen13.py`, `genranges13.sh`, `runfuzz13.sh`, `runmut13.sh`, `mutbuild13.sh`, `mkcast13.sh`, `castmodel13.sh`, `oraclevar13.sh`, `audit_table13.py`, `verdicts13.json`; the pins `TestRangeSetsRound13` and `TestSignHighBitRound13` in `Sh2DecompilerEngineTests.cpp` | fixed (instrument only; the defects are A2-82 to A2-87) | area 2 round 13 review | |
| A3-89 | 3 | P1 | A frame address a callee keeps is lost. Patch 0061 (`FrameEscape`) follows a frame address through the function's own ops, its own STOREs ((b)) and the output of a call it is an input of ((d)), and the host's `storesBelow` loop reads registers; neither sees a pointer a CALLEE wrote to memory. The call is no STORE of the function; once the stack is heritaged, the slot the callee was given a pointer to is the INDIRECT output of the call, which is no member of the post-heritage walk (`FrameEscape::compute(fd, spc, false)`, run by `AliasChecker::gatherInternal` and `reprocessFreeStores`), so `escaped` stays false, the alias boundary stays at the address that was passed, and the INDIRECTs of the walk's stores on the locals below it are collapsed; and the host's only record of it, `CallEffects::leaks`, sets `State::escaped` (what a later `Loaded` carries) and nothing the engine reads (`mEscaped` is set by a call's `storesBelow`/`storesThrough` registers and by the probe, never by a leak). So a frame address handed to a call that stores it -- in a global (a software stack's `init(top)`, a registered buffer) or through a second argument into a slot of the caller's frame (`get_cursor(&cur, buf_end)`) -- and a later step that reads it back, in the caller or in a second callee that reads the global, and stores below it (a pre-decrement walk, `*--p = v`, a negative displacement) writes the locals under the address the engine took to be reached only at and above it, and they are folded: lent, `return 5;`, the code returns 1. Five ways, one cause: (a) a lent g stores r4 to a global, a lent h loads the global and walks down from it; (b) the same with g not lent (the caller is withheld, the text stale); (c) a lent g stores its second argument through its first, a slot of the caller's frame, and the caller reloads the slot and walks down (loop) or stores at a constant negative displacement; (d) a lent g stores r4 to a global and a lent g2, which is handed nothing, returns the global in r0 (`return_global`), and the caller walks down from r0: the output of a call that was not handed the address, which clause (d) of 0061 does not cover; (e) an unlent captured callee that writes its own frame's word at its entry r15 + 4 with the address (`mov.l r4,@(4,r15)`, the caller's outgoing word), reloaded by the caller (the caller is withheld, the text stale). The shape is compiled: a stack-like container whose top is given at init, an out-parameter that receives a pointer into a buffer. Current (a): `xunknown4 func_0x06009000(void) { xunknown1 axStack_8 [4]; func_0x06009040(axStack_8); func_0x06009080(); return 5; }`, lent (known, 0 parameters); (c): `func_0x06009040(&piStack_4,axStack_8); iVar1 = 4; do { piStack_4 = piStack_4 + -1; *piStack_4 = iVar1; iVar1 = iVar1 + -1; } while (iVar1 != 0); return 5;`, lent. The emulator returns 1 (the walk reaches the sentinel at r15 + 0). Expected: the control's, `func_0x06009040(&iStack_8); return axStack_18[0];`-like, the local (A3-71a's text, which holds when the callee is handed the frame address directly). Oracle: `PseudoAgreesWithRun` rejects (a: "returns 00000005"; the loops of c are beyond its evaluator, the run says 1). The walk fuzzer `tools/a3_round12/walkfuzz3.py` (a frame address persisted by a callee through a global or an out-parameter slot, then walked down in the caller or in a second callee, 14 laundering steps and four walk forms before it): 796 of 796 programs stale in four seeds (459 of them lent), with the direct hand-over as the control 0 of 103 in three seeds (`CONTROL=1`); the generator draws none of it (`GenLaunderWalkFn`'s global step is a round trip inside one function). **Fix direction (not a fix):** for (c) and (e) the engine's post-heritage walk should take a stack word defined by the INDIRECT of a call that was handed a member as an unmodeled member (a diagnostic build of the review that adds every stack word defined by an INDIRECT to that walk prints `return xStack_18;` for c, with the store left marked or not; the same build leaves a, b, d unchanged). For the global (a, b, d) the engine has nothing to see: the host knows what a lent callee does with an address (`leaks`, `storesThrough` memory, `frameValues`), so a call whose callee leaks a value of stack origin (`map(e.leaks & ~kStack) & kStack`, the line that sets `State::escaped`) must also declare the host alias from the frame's lowest slot (`mEscaped`, `mEscapedLow`) when the function later calls a callee that stores through memory or reads memory it does not track; a callee that stores through a pointer it loaded from memory needs a `storesBelow` bit for memory, which the caller reads against `State::escaped`. Every one of these costs precision where a function passes the address of a local to a callee that keeps it; to be measured on the corpora. | **a** `CallFixture { 0x06009000, { 0x4F22, 0x7FEC, 0xE005, 0x2F02, 0x64F3, 0x7410, 0xD105, 0x410B, 0x0009, 0xD105, 0x410B, 0x0009, 0x60F2, 0x7F14, 0x4F26, 0x000B, 0x0009, 0x0009, 0x0600, 0x9040, 0x0600, 0x9080 } }` with the function at 0x06009040 `{ 0xD101, 0x2142, 0x000B, 0x0009, 0x0600, 0xF100 }` (decompiled first and lent when its signature is known), the function at 0x06009080 `{ 0xD103, 0x6112, 0xE204, 0x2126, 0x4210, 0x8BFC, 0x000B, 0x0009, 0x0600, 0xF100 }` (decompiled first and lent when its signature is known) (g: `mov.l @(lit),r1 ; | A3-90 | 3 | P2 | The probe's walk of an unlent captured callee records its `storesBelow` (A3-86 a: `CalleeStoresBelow`, read at the probe's own calls), but a walked callee that hands the pointer on to a callee of its own does not carry that callee's `storesBelow` up. `Call` maps a LENT callee's `storesBelow` transitively (A3-82) and a nested walk's thunk to a lent target (round 11), but in a nested walk (`mNested`) a call or tail jump to an unlent callee only maps its frame words (`MapCalleeWords(CalleeFrameWrites(target))`), and a call it cannot name (`jsr @r5`, a callback; a cycle or an exhausted budget, `kUnwalked`) maps nothing for `storesBelow` -- the top-level `!haveTarget` rule (a frame address in r4-r7 exposes the frame) is not applied to the nested walk. So `P -> U1 -> U2`, with U2 an unlent captured fill-backwards function (any reason: a hidden counter in r3) and U1 passing r4 on by `jsr`, by `jmp`, or by a call through a callback, leaves U1's `storesBelow` empty; P hands U1 a frame address, the engine aliases from the address up, and the read of the local after the call is folded: P is withheld ("it calls 0x06009040, whose signature is not known"), and its text is `return 5;` where the run returns 1. A function that is lent calls only lent functions, so only the probe's text is affected (the text of a withheld function is a P2, as A3-86 a). A one-level chain (U1 itself fills backwards) is right (A3-86 a); every longer chain is stale. Current (a, b and c alike): `xunknown4 func_0x06009000(void) { xunknown1 axStack_8 [4]; func_0x06009040(axStack_8); return 5; }`, withheld; the emulator returns 1. | A3-117 | 3 | P2 | Stack-argument words an unlent call takes for its own arguments are folded when the callee writes them through a pointer that escaped. A3-104's fix (patch 0065) aliases every Symbol from the frame's lowest offset up, but the words at the call's r15 that the engine prints as the call's fifth and later arguments are no Symbol (unmapped, `markNotMapped`; A3-99 keeps them out of the escape's alias so the arguments stay on the call), so a read of one after the call is the value stored before it. R13-1a with r6 set (the call is printed with a stack argument): the caller stores 1 to 4 in its four words, keeps `&local[2]` in an object through the unlent `g`, fills the buffer backwards through the kept pointer and returns local 0: `xVar2 = 1 ; func_0x06009100(&xStack_c,0x600f200,0x1f,in_r7,1,2) ; ... *(iRam0600f204 + -4) = 0x55 ; ... return xVar2;`, the code returns 0x55 (the text holds the walk's three stores and the argument list and states the folded value). The local above the argument words is right (R13-1a's own `return xStack_10`). Withheld (the callee is not lent), no note. The mechanism needs the call to show a stack argument: r6 or r7 or r5-r7 set with r4 holding the address (R14-7b to 7e); with only r4 and r5 the call has no stack argument and the text is right (R14-7a). It is the commonest wrong text of the old fuzzer: `escfuzz.py` seed 301 (1,500 fixtures) has 59 texts the oracle rejects, 56 of them returning a register variable of a frame the escape reaches (`REJECTS returns 00000001`), which round 13's summary did not count (it counted wrong constants only; the fixer's "0 of 15,400" does not cover them); `escsum14.py` counts them: 531 of 15,000 in seeds 1101 to 1110 of `escfuzz14.py`, every one with a call that shows a stack argument. Also wrong on 8dcbb70 and f54d140. Fix direction: keep the argument words out of the argument trial but not out of the escape: when the frame escaped, a call (unlent or whose effects name memory) may write every stack word of the frame, so a read after it of a word the call showed as an argument is a read of the word, not of the constant (an INDIRECT at the call for the words of its stack arguments when the escape is set, after the trial has decided the arguments); or, in the host, say it (the probe knows `mFrameOut` and the words the call shows). | `Docs/Decompiler/tools/a3_round14/r14e.src` R14-7b to 7e (controls 7a in `r14d.src`, 7f to 7i read the local above the argument words); `escfuzz14.py` (any seed; `escsum14.py` counts `REG-var stackarg-shown`: 531 of 15,000 in seeds 1101 to 1110). | A3-119 | 3 | P1 | A pointer walked down over the frame by a register stride the value-set analysis cannot read is not guarded below its base either: the same helper, from the other side. `pointerSteppedDown` starts at the store's pointer, which for `add r5,r2 ; mov.l r1,@r2` (or `sub r5,r2`) is the INT_ADD/INT_SUB of the loop's join and the stride, and returns false at it because the stride is not a constant; nothing then reaches the MULTIEQUAL that `AliasChecker::stepsDown` would call a step (a `sub` of a non-constant is one there). The value-set analysis reads a stride that is a copy of a constant or the result of an operation on one (`mov #-4,r5`, `neg`, `shll2`, `not`: R14-1a, R14-11 rows 1, 2, 5 to 7 are right); a stride loaded from a literal pool (`mov.w @(disp,pc),r5` for a stride over 127 bytes, `mov.l @(disp,pc),r5`), reloaded from a frame slot, or read from a global is a LOAD it cannot evaluate, and the range is full: the guard stays from the base up. R14-11 literal -4: `p = sp+16 ; r5 = -4 (pool long) ; L: add r5,r2 ; mov.l r1,@r2 ; dt r1 ; bf L` stores 4, 3, 2, 1 over sp+12 down to sp+0 and the code returns 1; lent `int4 f(void)`, `piVar2 = piVar2 + -1 ; *piVar2 = iVar1 ; ... return 5;` (the engine reads the stride at print time and shows the walk, and folds the sentinel). R14-14 (a table of ten stride sources, `add` and `sub`, constant and unbounded base, in a 0x200 frame): the pool halfword and long, the frame reload, the global, `neg` and `shll2` of a pool value, and `sub` of a pool or global positive stride are wrong in both bases (14 of 20, lent, `return 5;`), the constant copy and `sub #127` right. The stride of a pointer walked down by more than 127 bytes per step is compiled as a pool load (`mov.w @(disp,pc),r1 ; sub r1,r4`), so a loop over a local array of large structures from its end is the shape. The callee side is right: R14-12a to 12e (a lent `g(p)` that steps its parameter down by a pool stride) are `return xStack_1c` (the host's `StoresBelow` fails closed for an add of a variable). Fix direction: as A3-118's: `pointerSteppedDown` continues through an INT_ADD or INT_SUB with a non-constant operand to the join and asks `stepsDown` of it, and `stepsDown` takes an INT_ADD of a non-constant as a possible step down as it takes an INT_SUB (the cost is the guard below a loop that walks up by a variable stride: the same cost the `sub` case already pays). | A3-120 | 3 | P2 | (Notes, read against the code; P2 by the brief's rule that a false note is P2, P3 if the coordinator reads the notes as hazards.) A frame-fact note says "is" of a word or register the host only knows to be possible. (a) After a callee was handed an exact frame address in r4, the words it may store through that address are marked as possibly holding a frame address (`StoreThrough` with `CalleeStoresFrame`), and the next call whose stack words are those words is noted "a frame address is in a stack argument slot here, in none of the arguments: the call may keep it and write any local; likewise at the calls below". R14-16e: `g1(&buf) ; g2() ; g3() ; return buf[2]`, g1, g2, g3 all `rts ; nop`: no instruction of the function or of its callees ever writes a frame address anywhere, and the text says it at g2 (and "likewise" at g3). The control R14-16b (g1 handed 0) has no note; R14-16f (g1 alone) has none because no later call has the words. (b) Once anything went out, a register loaded from memory is a possible frame address (`MayBeFrame`), and a call's note says "a frame address is in r2 here" of it. Corpus, real entries: 008 06042BBC, the last call (`func_0x060062b4(iVar2 + 0x34)`, 06042D9A): r2 is `[r10+0x18]`, the object pointer the function loaded from its parameter (`mov.l @(0x18,r10),r2`; on every path to the call it was last written by that load or an add to it, never by `mov r14,r2`), and the new text says "a frame address is in r2 here, in none of the arguments"; the base said "a frame address is in a stack argument slot here" at the same call, equally unsupported. 007 0601B914 (`(*pcRam06000300)(0x4b,xRam0605830c)`, after `func_0x0602382c(axStack_10)` was handed the buffer): the same note as (a); the base printed it too. The note is right as a hazard (a callee handed `&buf` may have kept it) and wrong as a statement, and a reader cannot tell which. Proposal: say "a word the call may take for a frame address" when the flag comes from `StoreThrough`'s marking or from `MayBeFrame` of an unplaced value, and keep "is" for a value some instruction of the function made from r15 (`Value::Frame()` with `def != 0`, the test `mEscapeWords` already makes for the once-only rule). | `Docs/Decompiler/tools/a3_round14/r14o.src` R14-16 (the variant with a result stored), `r14q.src` R14-16e and 16f, R14-16b (control: no note) in `r14o.src`; the corpus functions with `SE_GAME_HWRAM=<hwram008.bin> SE_GAME_LEND=1 SE_GAME_OUT=<dir>` (`lend_after/06042BBC.c`; 007 `lend_after/0601B914.c`). Proposal: draw in the escape stream (a) an unlent call with r4 and one of r5-r7 set whose locals are the words at its r15, and (b) a walk whose pointer or index steps by a register loaded from the code's pool, from a slot or a global, and an index that counts down (`@(r0,p)`, `p - i`). Any other value's sign may decide. `mov r4,r0 ; mov r4,r1 ; cmp/pz r5 ; bt ; add #3,r1 ; mov.l @(pool),r2 ; and r2,r1 ; sub r1,r0 ; rts` (3 is added to a copy of r4 when r5 is negative, the copy is masked with ~3 and taken from r4) is printed `int4 func(int4 param_1) { return param_1 % 4; }`; for r4 = 1 and r5 = 0xFFFFFFFE the code returns 0xFFFFFFFD (1 - 4) and the text 1. The printed-C differential declares the wide name the engine makes for a write wider than its symbol (`_xStack_4 = CONCAT13(...)`, 4 bytes, since A2-57) and the narrow local that the same text reads afterwards (`xStack_4`, 2 bytes, the high half of that write) as two variables. | A2-96 | 2 | P3 | (Oracle.) The engine's reading of a text (`CxxHeader`) overloads `&&` and `\|\|` for its `T` types (`Bool4` is `T<4, false>`), so both operands are evaluated, and a text whose right operand assigns (`if ((param_1 == 0) && (param_1 = param_2, 0 < (int4)param_2))`, which the printer writes for a condition that sets a variable) runs the assignment on every path. The reading is then wrong where the C reading is right except for the 16-bit negation (`(int4)-param_3` of an `int2`, which C widens: the case `narrow-c` is for), and the function is reported `mismatch` instead of `narrow-c`. With the right operand nested in a second `if` the engine's reading agrees on all 32 inputs. 1 of 40,000 functions of family 78 (seeds 3,300,001 to 3,340,000). | `SE_GENERATED_PRINTEDC=only SE_GENERATED_PRINTEDC_WORDS="6043 6153 8800 8B0E 887F 8B06 3157 8901 707F 2009 4018 A005 0009 204A 4115 8B01 4119 4118 301C 211B 616B 210F 011A 000B 6013 0009"`: `mismatch (f0 r4=00000002 r5=00007FFF r6=FFFF8000 r7=00000000 size=4 run=FFFF0000:FFFF0000 pseudo=00010000:00000000)` in the C reading, `pseudo=C0008000` in the engine's. Expected: class `narrow-c`. The fix is a short-circuit `&&` and `\|\|` in the header (a lazily evaluated right operand) or the printer's comma form read as a statement. | open | area 2 round 14 review |  |
